38.06721pt\raise-33.07181pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.06721pt\raise-33.07181pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{({[R_{1}S_{11}]},{[S_{22}]})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

For example
({[S]},{[\epsilon]})\leq({[R_{1}S_{11}]},{[S_{22}]})

because
{[S]}\unlhd{[R_{1}S_{11}]}
, that is,

S/R_{1}S_{11}=S_{22}
is garbage,
and

\mathcal{G}({[S]}\hookrightarrow{[R_{1}S_{11}]})({[\epsilon]})={[S/R_{1}S_{11}]}={[S_{22}]}\sqsubseteq{[S_{22}]}
.

6
Conclusions

We have defined a calculus (
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
) based on
non-idempotent intersection types.
Its syntax and semantics are complex due to the presence of an admittedly
ad hoc
correctness invariant for terms,
enforced so that reduction is confluent.
In contrast, derivation spaces in this calculus turn out to be very simple structures:
they are representable as
rings of sets
(Prop.
5
)
and as a consequence they are distributive lattices (Coro.
1
).
Derivation spaces in the
\lambda
-calculus can be mapped to these
much simpler spaces using a strong notion of simulation (Coro.
2
)
inspired by residual theory. Building on this, we showed how the derivation space of any typable
\lambda
-term
may be factorized as a “twisted product” of garbage-free and garbage derivations (Thm.
5.1
).

We believe that this validates the (soft) hypothesis
that explicitly representing resource management can provide insight on the structure
of derivation spaces.

Related work.

The
Factorization
theorem (Thm.
5.1
) is reminiscent
of Melliès’ abstract factorization result
[
29
]
.
Given an axiomatic rewriting system fulfilling a number of axioms,
Melliès proves that every derivation can be uniquely
factorized as an
external
prefix followed by an
internal
suffix.
We conjecture that each refinement
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}t
should provide
an instantiation of Melliès’ axioms, in such a way that our

t^{\prime}
-garbage-free/
t^{\prime}
-garbage factorization coincides with his external/internal
factorization.
Melliès notes that any evaluation strategy that always selects external steps
is hypernormalizing. A similar result should hold for evaluation strategies that
always select
non-garbage
steps.

The notion of
garbage-free
derivation is closely related with the
notion of
X
-
neededness

[
3
]
.
A step
R
is
X
-needed
if every reduction to a term
t\in X
contracts a residual of
R
.
Recently, Kesner et al.
[
22
]
have related
typability in a non-idempotent intersection type system
\mathcal{V}

and weak-head neededness.
Using similar techniques, it should be possible to prove that

t^{\prime}
-garbage-free steps are
X
-needed,
where
X=\{s\ |\ s^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}s\}

and
s^{\prime}
is the
\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}
-normal form of
t^{\prime}
.

There are several resource calculi in the literature
which perhaps could play a similar role as
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
to recover factorization results akin to Thm.
5.1
.
Kfoury
[
23
]
embeds the
\lambda
-calculus
in a
linear

\lambda
-calculus that has no duplication nor erasure.
Ehrard and Regnier prove that the Taylor expansion of
\lambda
-terms
[
16
]

commutes with normalization, similarly as in
Algebraic Simulation
(Coro.
2
).
Mazza et al.
[
27
]
study a general framework for
polyadic approximations
,
corresponding roughly to the notion of
refinement
in this paper.

Acknowledgements.

To Eduardo Bonelli and Delia Kesner for introducing the first author to these topics.
To Luis Scoccola and the anonymous reviewers for helpful suggestions.

Appendix 0.A
Appendix

This technical appendix includes the detailed proofs of the results stated in the main body
of the paper that have been marked with the
\clubsuit
symbol.

0.A.1
Proof of Lem.
1
– Subject Reduction

We are to prove that
if
\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle
is correct
then
\mathtt{C}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
is correct,
and, moreover, that their unique typings are under the same typing context
and have the same type.
We need a few auxiliary results:

Lemma 6

If
\bar{s}
is a permutation of
\bar{u}
, then
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}=t\{x:=\bar{u}\}
.

Proof

By induction on
t
.

Lemma 7

If
t
is correct, then any subterm of
t
is correct.

Proof

Note, by definition of correctness (Def.
3
),
that if
t
is a correct abstraction
t=\lambda^{\ell}x.s
then
s
is correct,
and if
t
is a correct application
t=s[u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}]

then
s
and
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}
are correct.
This allows us to conclude by induction on
t
.

Lemma 8
(Relevance)

If
\Gamma\vdash t:\mathcal{A}

and
x\in\dom\Gamma
then
x\in\mathsf{fv}({t})
.

Proof

By induction on
t
.

Definition 13

\Lambda(t)
stands for the multiset of labels decorating the lambdas of
t
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Lambda(x^{\mathcal{A}})&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&[\,]\\
\Lambda(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&[\ell]+\Lambda(t)\\
\Lambda(t[s_{i}]_{i=1}^{n})&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&\Lambda(t)+_{i=1}^{n}\Lambda(s_{i})\\
\end{array}

For example,
\Lambda((\lambda^{1}x.x^{[\alpha^{2}]\overset{3}{\to}\alpha^{2}})[\lambda^{3}x.x^{\alpha^{2}}])=[1,3]
.

Lemma 9

Let
t,s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
be correct terms.
Then

\Lambda(t\{x:=[s_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\})=\Lambda(t)+_{i=1}^{n}\Lambda(s_{i})
.

Proof

By induction on
t
.

To prove Lem.
1
, we first show that substitution preserves typing,
and then that it preserves correctness.

Substitution preserves typing

More precisely, let us show that
if
\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle:\mathcal{A}
is derivable,
then
\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle:\mathcal{A}
is derivable.
By induction on the context
\mathtt{C}
.

1.

Empty,
\mathtt{C}=\Box
.

By induction on
t
.

1.1

Variable (same),
t=x^{\mathcal{A}}
,
\bar{s}=[s]
.

We have that
x:[\mathcal{A}]\vdash x^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}

and
\Delta\vdash s:\mathcal{B}
are derivable,
so we are done.

1.2

Variable (different),
t=y^{\mathcal{A}}
,
y\neq x
,
\bar{s}=[]
.

We have that
y:[\mathcal{A}]\vdash y^{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}
is derivable, so we are done.

1.3

Abstraction,
t=\lambda^{\ell}y.u
.

Let
\Gamma\oplus x:[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\vdash\lambda^{\ell}x.u:\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell}{\to}\mathcal{A}

be derivable
and
\Delta_{i}\vdash s_{i}:\mathcal{B}_{i}
be derivable for all
i=1..n
.
By inversion of the
lam
rule,
we have that
\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M}\oplus x:[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\vdash u:\mathcal{A}

is derivable, so by
i.h.

(\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M})+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\vdash u\{x:=[s_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\}:\mathcal{A}
is derivable.
Observe that
y\not\in\mathsf{fv}({s_{i}})
so
y\not\in\dom\Delta_{i}
by Lem.
8
.
Hence the previous judgment can be written as

(\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i})\oplus y:\mathcal{M}\vdash u\{x:=[s_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\}:\mathcal{A}
.
Applying the
lam
rule we obtain

\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\vdash\lambda^{\ell}y.u\{x:=[s_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\}:\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell}{\to}\mathcal{A}

as required.

1.4

Application,
t=u[r_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
.

Let
\Gamma\oplus x:[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\vdash u[r_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}:\mathcal{A}

be derivable
and
\Delta_{i}\vdash s_{i}:\mathcal{B}_{i}
be derivable for all
i=1..n
.
By inversion of the
app
rule,
the multiset of types
[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}

may be partitioned as
[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{M}_{j}
,
and
the typing context
\Gamma

may be partitioned as
\Gamma=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Gamma_{j}

in such a way that

\Gamma_{0}\oplus x:\mathcal{M}_{0}\vdash u:[\mathcal{C}_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\overset{\ell}{\to}\mathcal{A}

is derivable
and

\Gamma_{j}\oplus x:\mathcal{M}_{j}\vdash r_{j}:\mathcal{C}_{j}
is derivable for all
j=1..m
.
Consider a partition
(\bar{s}_{0},\ldots,\bar{s}_{j})

of the list
\bar{s}

such that for every
j=0..m
we have
\mathtt{T}(\bar{s}_{j})=\mathcal{M}_{j}
.
Observe that this partition exists since
\mathtt{T}(\bar{s}_{0}+\ldots+\bar{s}_{j})=\mathtt{T}(\bar{s})=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{M}_{j}=[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}=\mathtt{T}_{x}(t)
.

Moreover, let
\Theta_{j}=\sum_{i:s_{i}\in\bar{s}_{j}}\Delta_{i}
for all
j=0..m
.
By
i.h.
we have that

\Gamma_{0}+\Theta_{0}\vdash u\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}:[\mathcal{C}_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\overset{\ell}{\to}\mathcal{A}

is derivable
and

\Gamma_{j}+\Theta_{j}\vdash r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}:\mathcal{C}_{j}

is derivable for all
j=1..m
.
Applying the
app
rule we obtain
that

\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Gamma_{j}+\sum_{j=0}^{m}\Theta_{j}\vdash u[r_{j}]\{x:=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bar{s}_{j}\}:\mathcal{A}

is derivable.
By definition of

\Gamma_{0},\ldots,\Gamma_{m}
and

\Theta_{0},\ldots,\Theta_{m}

this judgment equals

\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\vdash u[r_{j}]\{x:=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\bar{s}_{j}\}:\mathcal{A}
.
By definition of
\bar{s}_{0},\ldots,\bar{s}_{m}

and Lem.
6

this in turn equals

\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\vdash u[r_{j}]\{x:=\bar{s}\}:\mathcal{A}
,
as required.

2.

Under an abstraction,
\mathtt{C}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

3.

Left of an application,
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\bar{u}
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

4.

Right of an application,
\mathtt{C}=u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime},\bar{r}_{2}]
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

Substitution preserves correctness

More precisely, let us show that
if
\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle
is correct
then
\mathtt{C}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
is correct.
By induction on
\mathtt{C}
:

1.

Empty,
\mathtt{C}=\Box
.

Let
\bar{s}=[s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}]
.
Observe that if
(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)[s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}]
is correct then:

•

[c1]

\Gamma\oplus x:[\mathcal{B}_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}\vdash t:\mathcal{A}
and
\Delta_{i}\vdash s_{i}:\mathcal{B}_{i}
are derivable for all
i=1..n
,

•

[c2]

t,s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
are correct,

•

[c3]
there are no free occurrences of
x
among
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
,

•

[c4]
all the lambdas occurring in
t,s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
have pairwise distinct labels,

•

[c5]

\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}
is a sequential context.

Condition
[c1]
holds by inversion of the typing rules,
condition
[c2]
holds by Lem.
7
,
condition
[c3]
holds by Barendregt’s convention,
and conditions
[c4]
and
[c5]
hold because the source term is supposed to be correct.

By induction on
t
, we check that
if
(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)[s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}]

is correct, then

t\{x:=\bar{s}\}
is correct.

1.1

Variable (same),
t=x^{\mathcal{A}}
,
\bar{s}=[s]
.

Note that
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}=s
. Conclude by
[c2]
.

1.2

Variable (different),
t=y^{\mathcal{A}}
,
\bar{s}=[]
with
x\neq y
.

Note that
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}=y^{\mathcal{A}}
.
Conclude by
[c2]
.

1.3

Abstraction,
t=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u
.

Then
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}
and
by inversion
\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M}\vdash u:\mathcal{C}
is derivable.
Note that
(\lambda^{\ell}x.u)\bar{s}
is correct,
so by
i.h.

u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
is correct.
The variable
y
does not occur free in
\bar{s}
,
so
(\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M})+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}=(\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i})\oplus(y:\mathcal{M})
.
Let us check that
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
is correct:

1.3.1

Uniquely labeled lambdas.

Let
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
be two labels decorating different lambdas of

\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
,
and let us show that
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
.
There are two subcases, depending on whether one of the labels
decorates the outermost lambda:

1.3.1.1

If
\ell_{1}
or
\ell_{2}
decorates the outermost lambda.

Suppose without loss of generality that
\ell_{1}=\ell^{\prime}
is the label decorating the outermost lambda.
Then by Lem.
9
,
there are two cases: either
\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda of
u
,
or
\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda of some term in the list
\bar{s}
.
If
\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda of
u
, then
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}

since we knew that
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u
was a correct term by
[c2]
.
If
\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda of some term in the list
\bar{s}
, then
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}

by condition
[c4]
.

1.3.1.2

If
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
do not decorate the outermost lambda.

Then
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
decorate different lambdas of the term
u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
,
and we conclude by
i.h.
.

1.3.2

Sequential contexts.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}x.u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
, we conclude by
i.h.
.
Otherwise
t^{\prime}
is the whole term and the context is
\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}
,
which is sequential by hypothesis
[c5]
.

1.3.3

Sequential types.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}x.u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
u\{x:=\bar{s}\}
, we conclude by
i.h.
.
Otherwise
t^{\prime}
is the whole term. Then
\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\vdash t^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}
is derivable,
since we have already shown that substitution preserves typing.
Let
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}
be
a type such that
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}
or
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\mathcal{A}
,
and let us show that
\mathcal{N}
is sequential.
In the first case,
i.e.
if
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\Gamma+_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}
holds,
then either
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\Gamma
or
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\Delta_{i}
for some
i=1..n
,
and we have that
\mathcal{N}
is sequential because all the terms
t,s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
are correct by
[c2]
.
In the second case,
i.e.
if
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{D}\preceq\mathcal{A}
holds,
then we have that
\mathcal{N}
is sequential because
t
is correct by
[c2]
.

1.4

Application,
t=u\,\bar{r}
.

Let
\bar{r}=[r_{1},\ldots,r_{m}]
.
Note that
(\lambda^{\ell}x.u)\bar{s}_{0}
is correct
and
(\lambda^{\ell}x.r_{j})\bar{s}_{j}
is correct for all
j=1..m
,
which means that we may apply the
i.h.
in all these cases.
Let us show that
u[r_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\{x:=\bar{s}\}
is correct:

1.4.1

Uniquely labeled lambdas.

Let
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
be two labels decorating different lambdas of

u[r_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\{x:=\bar{s}\}
, and let us show that
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
.
Observe that the term

u[r_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\{x:=\bar{s}\}=u\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}[r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}]_{j=1}^{m}

has
m+1
immediate subterms, namely

u\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}

and
r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}
for each
j=1..m
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}

decorate two lambdas in the same immediate subterm or in different immediate subterms.

1.4.1.1

The labels
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
decorate the same immediate subterm.

That is,
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
both decorate lambdas in
u\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}

or both decorate lambdas in some
r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}
for some
j=1..m
.
Then we conclude, since both
u\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}
and
the
r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}
are correct by
i.h.
.

1.4.1.2

The labels
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
decorate different subterms.

Let
r_{0}:=u
.
Then we have that

\ell_{1}
decorates a lambda in
r_{j}\{x:=\bar{s}_{j}\}
for some
j=0..m

and

\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda in
r_{k}\{x:=\bar{s}_{k}\}
for some
k=0..m
,
j\neq k
.
By Lem.
9
,

\ell_{1}
decorates a lambda in
r_{j}
or a lambda in a term of the list
\bar{s}_{j}
,
and similarly

\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda in
r_{k}
or a lambda in a term of the list
\bar{s}_{k}
.
This leaves four possibilities, which are all consequence of
[c4]
.

1.4.2

Sequential contexts.

Similar to item
1.3.2
.

1.4.3

Sequential types.

Similar to item
1.3.3
.

2.

Under an abstraction,
\mathtt{C}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.

Note that
\Gamma\vdash\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle:\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{A}
is derivable.
Let us check the three conditions to see that
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
is correct:

2.1

Uniquely labeled lambdas.

Any two lambdas in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
have different labels by
i.h.
.
We are left to check that
\ell^{\prime}
does not decorate any lambda in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
.
Let
\ell_{1}
be a label that decorates a lambda in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
.
Then we have that
\ell_{1}
decorates a lambda in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
,
or it decorates a lambda in
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}
.
By what we proved in item
1

this in turn means that it decorates a lambda in
t
or a lambda in some of the terms of the list
\bar{s}
.
In any of these cases we have that
\ell_{1}\neq\ell^{\prime}

since
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle
is correct.

2.2

Sequential contexts.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle

and let us check that its typing context is sequential.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
we conclude by
i.h.
.
We are left to check the property for
t^{\prime}
being the whole term,
i.e.
that
\Gamma
is sequential.
By
i.h.
,
\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M}
is sequential, which implies that
\Gamma
is sequential.

2.3

Sequential types.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle

and let us check that, if
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}
is any type occurring in the typing
context or in the type of
t^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{N}
is sequential.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
we conclude by
i.h.
.
We are left to check the property for
t^{\prime}
being the whole term.

If
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\Gamma
,
then
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{N}

which is the type of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
,
so by
i.h.

\mathcal{N}
is sequential.

If
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{A}
,
there are three subcases:

2.3.1

If
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{M}
, then note that
\mathcal{M}
is sequential
because
\Gamma\oplus y:\mathcal{M}
is the typing context of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
,
which is sequential by
i.h.
.

2.3.2

If
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\mathcal{B}
where
\mathcal{B}
is one of the types of
\mathcal{M}
,
then
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\mathcal{M}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{A}
which is the typing context
of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
,
and we conclude for this term has sequential types by
i.h.
.

2.3.3

If
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\mathcal{A}
,
note that
\mathcal{A}
is the type of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
,
and we conclude for this term has sequential types by
i.h.
.

3.

Left of an application,
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\bar{u}
.

Note that
\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle:[\mathcal{B}_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{A}

is derivable.
Moreover the list of arguments is of the form

\bar{u}=[u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}]
where all the
u_{j}
are correct
and
\Delta_{j}\vdash u_{j}:\mathcal{B}_{j}
is derivable for all
j=1..m
.
Then
\Gamma+_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}:\mathcal{A}

is derivable.
Let us check the three conditions to see that
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
is correct:

3.1

Uniquely labeled lambdas.

Let
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
be two labels decorating different lambdas in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
.
There are three subcases.

3.1.1

If
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
both decorate lambdas in the subterm
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle

then
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
since
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
is correct by
i.h.
.

3.1.2

If
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
both decorate lambdas somewhere in
[u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}]

then
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
since
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\rangle[u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}]
is correct by hypothesis.

3.1.3

If
\ell_{1}
decorates a lambda in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle

and
\ell_{2}
decorates a lambda in one of the terms
u_{j}
for some
j=1..m
,
then note that
\ell_{1}
must either decorate a lambda in
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
or a lambda in
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}
.
By what we proved in item
1

this in turn means that it decorates a lambda in
t
or a lambda in some of the terms of the list
\bar{s}
.
In any of these cases we have that
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}

since
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
is correct by hypothesis.

3.2

Sequential contexts.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}

and let us show that its typing context is sequential.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
we conclude by
i.h.
.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of one of the
u_{j}
for some
j=1..m
, we conclude using that
u_{j}
is correct by hypothesis.
It remains to check that the whole term is correct,
i.e.
that
\Gamma+_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}
is sequential.
Observe that
\Gamma+_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}
is also the typing context of

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
, which is correct by hypothesis.

3.3

Sequential types.

Let
t^{\prime}
be a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}

and let us show if
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}
is a type
that occurs in the typing context or in the type of
t^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{N}
is sequential.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
we conclude by
i.h.
.
If
t^{\prime}
is a subterm of one of the
u_{j}
for some
j=1..m
, we conclude using that
u_{j}
is correct by hypothesis.
We are left to check the property for
t^{\prime}
being the whole term.

If
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\Gamma+_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}
,
we conclude by observing
that
\Gamma+_{j=1}^{m}\Delta_{j}
is also the typing context of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
,
which is correct by hypothesis, so it has sequential types.

Similarly, if
\mathcal{N}\overset{\ell^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{C}\preceq\mathcal{A}
,
we conclude by observing
that
\mathcal{A}
is also the type of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[u_{j}]_{j=1}^{m}
.

4.

Right of an application,
\mathtt{C}=u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime},\bar{r}_{2}]
.

Similar to item
3
.

0.A.2
Proof of Lem.
2
– Substitution Lemma

Let us prove that

t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\{y:=\bar{u}\}=t\{y:=\bar{u}_{1}\}\{x:=\bar{s}\{y:=\bar{u}_{2}\}\}

by induction on
t
.
The interesting case is the application.
Let
t=r[r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}]
. Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}&&(r[r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}])\{x:=\bar{s}\}\{y:=\bar{u}\}\\
&=&(r\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}[r_{i}\{x:=\bar{s}_{i}\}]_{i=0}^{n})\{y:=\bar{u}\}\\
&=&r\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\}\{y:=\bar{u}_{0}\}[r_{i}\{x:=\bar{s}_{i}\}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i}\}]_{i=0}^{n}\\
&\overset{\mathrm{h.i.}}{=}&r\{y:=\bar{u}_{0,1}\}\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\{y:=\bar{u}_{0,2}\}\}[r_{i}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i,1}\}\{x:=\bar{s}_{0}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i,2}\}\}]_{i=0}^{n}\\
&=&(r\{y:=\bar{u}_{0,1}\}[r_{i}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i,1}\}]_{i=0}^{n})\{x:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{s}_{i}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i,2}\}\}\\
\end{array}

Given that
\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{s}_{i}
is a permutation of
\bar{s}
,
by defining
\bar{v}_{2}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i,2}
,
the last term equals

(r\{y:=\bar{u}_{0,1}\}[r_{i}\{y:=\bar{u}_{i,1}\}]_{i=0}^{n})\{x:=\bar{s}\{y:=\bar{v}_{2}\}\}
.
Finally, by defining
\bar{v}_{1}:=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i,1}
,
we obtain

(r[r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}])\{y:=\bar{v}_{1}\}\{x:=\bar{s}\{y:=\bar{v}_{2}\}\}
.
To conclude observe that
\bar{v}_{1}+\bar{v}_{2}
is indeed a permutation of
\bar{u}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\bar{v}_{1}+\bar{v}_{2}&=&(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i,1})+(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i,2})\\
&\approx&\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i,1}+\bar{u}_{i,2}\\
&\approx&\sum_{i=0}^{n}\bar{u}_{i}&\text{by {i.h.} on each index $i=0..n$}\\
&=&\bar{u}\end{array}

where
\bar{x}\approx\bar{y}
is the equivalence relation on lists that holds whenever
\bar{x}
is a permutation of
\bar{y}
.

0.A.3
Proof of Prop.
2
– Permutation

Let us extend the operation of substitution to operate on contexts
by declaring that
\mathtt{T}_{x}(\Box)=[\,]

and
\Box\{x:=[\,]\}=\Box
.
We need two auxiliary lemmas:

Lemma 10
(Substitution lemma for contexts)

If both sides of the equation are defined
and
(\bar{s}_{1},\bar{s}_{2})
is a partition of
\bar{s}

then

\mathtt{C}\langle t\rangle\{x:=\bar{s}\}=\mathtt{C}\{x:=\bar{s_{1}}\}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}_{2}\}\rangle
.

Proof

The proof is similar to the Substitution Lemma, by induction on
\mathtt{C}
.

Lemma 11
(Reduction inside a substitution)

Let
1\leq i\leq n
and
s_{i}\overset{\ell}{\to}s^{\prime}_{i}
.
Then:

t\{x:=[s_{1},\ldots,s_{i-1},s_{i},s_{i+1},\ldots,s_{n}]\}\overset{\ell}{\to}t\{x:=[s_{1},\ldots,s_{i-1},s^{\prime}_{i},s_{i+1},\ldots,s_{n}]\}

.

Proof

Straightforward by induction on
s
.

The proof of Prop.
2
proceeds as follows.
Let
R:t_{0}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\ell}_{\texttt{{\#}}}}t_{1}
and
S:t_{0}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\ell^{\prime}}_{\texttt{{\#}}}}t_{2}

be coinitial steps, and let us show that the diagram may be closed.
The step
R
is of the form

t_{0}=\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\ell}_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle=t_{1}
.
Recall that
R\neq S
by hypothesis.
We proceed by induction on
\mathtt{C}
.

1.

Empty context,
\mathtt{C}=\Box
.

Then

R:t_{0}=(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\mathrel{\xrightarrow{\ell}_{\texttt{{\#}}}}t\{x:=\bar{s}\}=t_{1}
.
There are two subcases, depending on whether the pattern of
S
is inside
t

or inside
\bar{s}
:

1.1

The pattern of
S
is in
t
.

Using Lem.
10
, the situation is:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}\rangle)\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}\langle r\{y:=\bar{u}\}\rangle)\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}\rangle\{x:=\bar{s}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}\langle r\{y:=\bar{u}\}\rangle\{x:=\bar{s}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}\{x:=\bar{s}_{1}\}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r\{x:=\bar{s}_{2}\})\bar{u}\{x:=\bar{s}_{3}\}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}\{x:=\bar{s}_{1}\}\langle r\{y:=\bar{u}\}\{x:=\bar{s}_{4}\}\rangle}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}\{x:=\bar{s}_{1}\}\langle r\{x:=\bar{s}_{2}\}\{y:=\bar{u}\{x:=\bar{s}_{3}\}\}\rangle}

where
(\bar{s}_{1},\bar{s}_{2},\bar{s}_{3})
and
(\bar{s}_{1},\bar{s}_{4})
are partitions of
\bar{s}
.
Note that
(\bar{s}_{2},\bar{s}_{3})
is a partition of
\bar{s}_{4}
,
so it suffices to show that

r\{y:=\bar{u}\}\{x:=\bar{s}_{4}\}=r\{x:=\bar{s}_{2}\}\{y:=\bar{u}\{x:=\bar{s}_{3}\}\}
, which is an immediate consequence of the Substitution Lemma (Lem.
2
).

1.2

The pattern of
S
is inside
\bar{s}
.

In this case,
\bar{s}=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]
.

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)[\bar{s}_{1},r\{y:=\bar{u}\},\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{t\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{t\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},r\{y:=\bar{u}\},\bar{s}_{2}]\}}

The arrow of the bottom of the diagram exists as a consequence of Lem.
11
.

2.

Under an abstraction,
\mathtt{C}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

3.

Left of an application,
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\bar{u}
.

There are three subcases, depending on whether the redex
S
is
at the root, to the left of the application, or to the right of the application.

3.1

The pattern of
S
is at the root
.
Then
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle
must have a lambda at the root,
so it is of the form
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle
.
Hence, the starting term is

(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle)\bar{u}
.
The symmetric case has already been studied in item
1.1
.

3.2

The pattern of
S
is inside
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.
Straightforward by
i.h.
.

3.3

The pattern of
S
is inside
\bar{u}
.

It is immediate to close the diagram since the steps are at disjoint positions:

\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{p}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle r\{y:=\bar{p}\}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{p}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle r\{y:=\bar{p}\}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]}

4.

Right of an application,

\mathtt{C}=r[u_{1},\ldots,u_{i-1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime},u_{i+1},\ldots,u_{n}]
.
There are four subcases, depending on whether the redex
S
is
at the root, to the left of the application (that is, inside
r
),
or to the right of the application (that is, either inside
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
or
u_{j}
for some
j
).

4.1

The pattern of
S
is at the root.

Then
r
has a lambda at the root,
i.e.
it is of the form
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u
.
Hence the starting term is

(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)[u_{1:i-1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)s\rangle,u_{i+1:n}]
.
The symmetric case has already been studied in item
1.2
.

4.2

The pattern of
S
is inside
r
.

The steps are disjoint, so it is immediate.

4.3

The pattern of
S
is inside
u_{j}
for some
j\neq i
.

The steps are disjoint, so it is immediate.

4.4

The pattern of
S
is inside
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

0.A.4
Full Stability

In this section, we state and prove a strong version of Stability
in the sense of Lévy
[
26
]
,
called Full Stability (Lem.
15
).
This lemma is crucial in establishing that

\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t)
forms a distributive lattice (Prop.
4
).
First we need a few auxiliary results:

Definition 14
(Alternative Substitution)

An alternative definition for capture-avoiding substitution,
written
t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
,
may be defined as follows,
provided that

\mathtt{T}_{x}(t)\subseteq\mathtt{T}(\bar{s})

and the multiset of types of
\bar{s}=[s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}]

is sequential:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}x^{\mathcal{A}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&s_{i}&\text{ where $i$ is the unique index such that}\\
&&&\text{ $\mathtt{T}(s_{i})$ is the external label of $\mathcal{A}$}\\
y^{\mathcal{A}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&y^{\mathcal{A}}&\text{ if $x\neq y$}\\
(\lambda^{\ell}y.t)\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&\lambda^{\ell}y.t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&\text{ if $x\neq y$ and $y\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\bar{s}})$}\\
(t[u_{i}]_{i=1}^{k})\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}&t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}[u_{i}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}]_{i=1}^{k}\end{array}

Moreover,

[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}

stands for
[t_{1}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\},\ldots,t_{n}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}]
,
whenever each substitution
t_{i}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
is well-defined.

Lemma 12

Let
t,\bar{s},\bar{u}
be correct terms such that

\mathtt{T}_{x}(t)=\mathtt{T}(\bar{s})
and
suppose that
\bar{s}
is contained in
\bar{u}
,
i.e. there exists a list
\bar{r}
such that

(\bar{s},\bar{r})
is a partition of
\bar{u}
.
Then
t\{x:=\bar{s}\}=t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}
.

Proof

Straightforward by induction on
t
.

Lemma 13
(Creation)

If
R
creates
S
then it is according to
one of the following cases:

1.

[crI]
:

\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.x^{\mathcal{A}})\,[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.t]\,\bar{s}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.t)\,\bar{s}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle t\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle
.

2.

[crII]
:

\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.t)\,\bar{s}\,\bar{u}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.t^{\prime})\,\bar{u}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle t^{\prime}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}\rangle
, where

t^{\prime}=t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.

3.

[crIII]
:

\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{t}\rangle)\bar{s}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\,\bar{t^{\prime}}\rangle\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{t^{\prime}}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle
,
where

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathtt{C}_{2}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&=&\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\\
x^{\mathcal{A}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&=&\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u\\
\bar{t}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}&=&\bar{t^{\prime}}\\
\bar{s}&=&[\bar{s_{1}},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u,\bar{s_{2}}]\\
\end{array}

Proof

Let
R:\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle
be a step,
and let
S:\mathtt{C}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}p
another step
such that
R
creates
S
.
The redex contracted by the step
S
is below a context
\mathtt{C}_{1}
,
so let
\mathtt{C}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle
,
where
(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}
is the redex contracted by
S
.
We consider three cases, depending on the relative positions of the holes of
\mathtt{C}
and
\mathtt{C}_{1}
,
namely they may be disjoint,

\mathtt{C}
may be a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
,
or
\mathtt{C}_{1}
may be a prefix of
\mathtt{C}
:

1.

If the holes of
\mathtt{C}
and
\mathtt{C}_{1}
are disjoint.

Then there is a two-hole context
\hat{\mathtt{C}}
such that

\mathtt{C}=\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle\Box,(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle
and
\mathtt{C}_{1}=\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\},\Box\rangle
.
So

\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\},u\{\!\!\{y:=r\}\!\!\}\rangle
.
Observe that
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

2.

If
\mathtt{C}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
.

Then there exists a context
\mathtt{C}_{2}
such that
\mathtt{C}_{1}=\mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\rangle
,
and we have that

t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}=\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle
.
We consider two subcases, depending on whether the position of the hole
\mathtt{C}_{2}
lies
inside the term
t
, or it reaches a free occurrence of
x
in
t

and “jumps” to one of the arguments in the list
\bar{s}
.

2.1

If the position of the hole of
\mathtt{C}_{2}
lies in
t
.

More precisely, there is a context
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}
and a term
t^{\prime}
such that:

t=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle t^{\prime}\rangle
,

\mathtt{C}_{2}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
,
and

(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}=t^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
We consider three further subcases for the term
t^{\prime}
.
It cannot be an abstraction, so it may be a variable
x^{\mathcal{A}}
,
or an application.
Besides, if it is an application, the head may be a variable
x^{\mathcal{A}}
or an abstraction.

2.1.1

Variable,
i.e.

t^{\prime}=x^{\mathcal{A}}
.

Then the list
\bar{s}
may be split as
\bar{s}=[\bar{s_{1}},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r},\bar{s_{2}}]
,
and:

\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle)[\bar{s_{1}},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r},\bar{s_{2}}]\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle
.
Observe that
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

2.1.2

Application of a variable,
i.e.

t^{\prime}=x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{r^{\prime}}
.

Given that

(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}=t^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
,
we have
\bar{r}=\bar{r^{\prime}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
,
and the list
\bar{s}
may be split as
\bar{s}=[\bar{s_{1}},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u,\bar{s_{2}}]
.
Then

\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}^{\prime}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{r^{\prime}}\rangle)[\bar{s_{1}},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u,\bar{s_{2}}]\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\,\bar{r}\rangle\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle
,
and we are in the situation of
[crIII]
.

2.1.3

Application of an abstraction,
i.e.

t^{\prime}=(\lambda^{\ell}y.u^{\prime})\,\bar{r^{\prime}}
.

Given that

(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}=t^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
,
we have
\bar{u}=\bar{u^{\prime}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}

and
\bar{r}=\bar{r^{\prime}}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
Then:

\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}^{\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}y.u^{\prime})\,\bar{r^{\prime}}\rangle)\bar{s}\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}y.u)\,\bar{r}\rangle\rangle\ \mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\ \mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle u\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle
.
Then
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

2.2

If the position of the hole of
\mathtt{C}_{2}
“jumps” through a free occurrence of
x
.

More precisely,
there exist
\mathtt{C}_{3}
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\bar{s_{1}}
,
\bar{s_{2}}
, and
\mathtt{C}_{4}

such that:

t=\mathtt{C}_{3}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle\hskip 14.22636pt\bar{s}=[\bar{s_{1}},\mathtt{C}_{4}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle,\bar{s_{2}}]

in such a way that
\mathtt{T}(\mathtt{C}_{4}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle)=\mathtt{T}_{x}(x^{\mathcal{A}})
,
and
\mathtt{C}_{2}=\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\langle\mathtt{C}_{4}\rangle
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{3}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle)[\bar{s_{1}},\mathtt{C}_{4}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle,\bar{s_{2}}]\rangle\\
\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\langle\mathtt{C}_{4}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}\rangle\rangle\rangle\\
\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}\langle\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\langle\mathtt{C}_{4}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle\end{array}

Then
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

3.

If
\mathtt{C}_{1}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}
.

Then there exists a context
\mathtt{C}_{2}
such that
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}\rangle
.
This means that
\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle=(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\bar{r}
.
We consider three subcases, depending on whether
\mathtt{C}_{2}
is empty,
or the hole of
\mathtt{C}_{2}
lies to the left or to the right of the application.

3.1

Empty,
\mathtt{C}_{2}=\Box
.

Then
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}_{1}
so
\mathtt{C}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
. We have already considered this case.

3.2

Left,
\mathtt{C}_{2}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\,\bar{r}
.

Then the step
R
is of the form

R:\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle\,\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle\,\bar{r}\rangle
.
In particular we know that
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u
.
There are two cases, depending on whether
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}
is empty or non-empty.

3.2.1

Empty,
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\Box

Then we have that
t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u
.
We consider two further subcases, depending on whether
t
is an occurrence of the variable
x
.

3.2.1.1

If
t=x^{\mathcal{A}}
for some type
\mathcal{A}
.

Then the list
\bar{s}
must be of the form
[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u]

where the external label of
\mathcal{A}
is precisely
\ell^{\prime}
.
Then

\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.x^{\mathcal{A}})[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u]\,\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\,\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle
,
and we are in the situation of
[crI]
.

3.2.1.2

If
t\neq x^{\mathcal{A}}
for any type
\mathcal{A}
.

Then
t
must be an abstraction, namely
t=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u^{\prime}

where
u^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}=u
.
Then

\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u^{\prime})\,\bar{s}\,\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\})\,\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle u^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle
,
and we are in the situation of
[crII]
.

3.2.2

Non-empty,
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\neq\Box

Then necessarily
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}
must be an abstraction, namely
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\,\bar{s}\rangle)\,\bar{r}\rangle&\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle)\,\bar{r}\rangle\\
&\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{r}\}\!\!\}\rangle\end{array}

Then
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

3.3

Right,
\mathtt{C}_{2}=(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2},\bar{r}_{2}]
.

Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\,[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\rangle&\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u)\,[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\rangle\\
&\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}&\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle u\{\!\!\{y:=[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\end{array}

Then
S
has an ancestor
S_{0}
, contradicting the hypothesis that
R
creates
S
.
So this case is impossible.

Lemma 14
(Basic Stability)

Let
R\neq S
.
If
T_{3}\in T_{1}/(S/R)

and
T_{3}\in T_{2}/(R/S)

then there exists a step
T_{0}
such that

T_{1}\in T_{0}/R

and

T_{2}\in T_{0}/S
.

Proof

We prove an equivalent statement.
Namely,
we prove that if
R,S
are different coinitial steps such that

R
creates a step
T
,
then
R/S
creates the step
T/(S/R)
.
It is easy to see that these are equivalent since in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
there is
no erasure or duplication.

Let
R:\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}\langle t\{x:=\bar{s}\}\rangle
,
let
S\neq R
be a step coinitial to
R
,
and suppose that
R
creates a step
T
.
By induction on the context
\mathtt{C}
we argue that
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
.

1.

Empty context,
\mathtt{C}=\Box
.

Then
R:(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\bar{s}\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}t\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
There are two cases for
S
, depending on whether it is internal to
t

or internal to one of the arguments of the list
\bar{s}
.

1.1

If
S
is internal to
t
.

Then
t=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle
where
\Sigma
is the redex contracted by
S
.
Let
\Sigma^{\prime}
denote the contractum of
\Sigma
. Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}

By Creation (Lem.
13
),

T
must be created by
[crIII]
,
since there are no applications surrounding the subterm contracted by
R
.
This means that
\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle=\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle

where, moreover,
\bar{s}
may be split as
[\bar{s}_{1},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r,\bar{s}_{2}]

in such a way that
\ell^{\prime}
is the external label of
\mathcal{A}
.
We consider three subcases, depending on whether the contexts

\mathtt{C}_{1}
and
\mathtt{C}_{2}
are disjoint,

\mathtt{C}_{1}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{2}
,
or
\mathtt{C}_{2}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
.

1.1.1

If
\mathtt{C}_{1}
and
\mathtt{C}_{2}
are disjoint.

Then there is a two-hole context
\hat{\mathtt{C}}
such that

\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle\Box,x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle=\mathtt{C}_{1}
and
\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle\Sigma,\Box\rangle=\mathtt{C}_{2}
.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let us write
\mathtt{X}^{*}
to denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
Then
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle\Sigma,x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\hat{\mathtt{C}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{*},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\hat{\mathtt{C}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{*},r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}\rangle}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\hat{\mathtt{C}}\langle\Sigma^{\prime},x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\hat{\mathtt{C}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{\prime*},(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{\hat{\mathtt{C}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{\prime*},r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle}

1.1.2

If
\mathtt{C}_{1}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{2}
.

Then
\mathtt{C}_{2}=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\rangle
which means that
\Sigma=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle
.
Recall that
\Sigma
is a redex, so let us write

\Sigma=(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p)\bar{q}
.
We consider two further subcases, depending on whether the hole of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}

lies to the left or to the right of the application (observe that it cannot be at the root
since
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p
is not a variable).

1.1.2.1

If the hole of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}
lies to the left of
(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p)\bar{q}
.

More precisely, we have that

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}=(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\bar{q}
and
p=\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle
.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let us write
\mathtt{X}^{*}
to denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
Then
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle)\bar{q}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}^{*}\rangle)\bar{q}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle)\bar{q}^{*}\rangle}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\bar{u}\rangle\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}\}\!\!\}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle}

1.1.2.2

If the hole of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}
lies to the right of
(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p)\bar{q}
.

More precisely, we have that
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{2}=(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p)[\bar{q}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2},\bar{q}_{2}]

and
\bar{q}=[\bar{q}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle,\bar{q}_{2}]
.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let us write
\mathtt{X}^{*}
to denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
Moreover, since parameters are in 1–1 correspondence with arguments,
there is exactly one free occurrence of
z
in
p
whose type coincides with
the type of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle
.
Let
p=\mathtt{C}_{3}\langle z^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle
where the hole of
\mathtt{C}_{3}
marks the position
of such occurrence.
Then:

\mathtt{C}_{3}\langle z^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}=\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}\langle(\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle)^{*}\rangle=\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}\langle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{u}^{*}\rangle)\rangle

Moreover, if we write
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{3}
for the context
\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}\}\!\!\}
:

(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{3})^{*}=(\mathtt{C}_{3}\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}\}\!\!\})^{*}=\mathtt{C}_{3}^{*}\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{q}^{*}\}\!\!\}

by the Substitution Lemma for contexts (Lem.
10
).
Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.\mathtt{C}_{3}\langle z^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle)\bar{q}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.\mathtt{C}_{3}^{*}\langle z^{\mathcal{B}}\rangle)\bar{q}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{3}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\,\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle(\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{3})^{*}\langle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\rangle\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}

The labels of the steps
T
and
T/(S/R)
are both
\ell^{\prime}
,
which means that
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
.

1.1.3

If
\mathtt{C}_{2}
is a prefix of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
.

Then
\mathtt{C}_{1}=\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle

which means
x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle
.
Since
\Sigma
is a redex,
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}
must be of the form
x^{\mathcal{A}}\,[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\bar{u}_{2}]

and
\bar{u}=[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]
.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let us write
\mathtt{X}^{*}
to denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}
.
Then:
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]\rangle)\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)[\bar{u}_{1}^{*},{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{*}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}^{*}]\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=[\bar{u}_{1}^{*},{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{*}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}^{*}]\}\!\!\}\rangle}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}[\bar{u}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}]\rangle)\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)[\bar{u}_{1}^{*},{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{\prime*}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}^{*}]\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=[\bar{u}_{1}^{*},{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{*}\langle\Sigma^{\prime*}\rangle,\bar{u}_{2}^{*}]\}\!\!\}\rangle}

1.2

If
S
is internal to
\bar{s}
.

Then
\bar{s}=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]

where
\Sigma
is the redex contracted by
S
.
Let
\Sigma^{\prime}
denote the contractum of
\Sigma
. Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\,[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{t\{\!\!\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\}\!\!\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.t)\,[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{t\{\!\!\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\}\!\!\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}

By Creation (Lem.
13
),
T
must be created by

[crIII]
,
since there are no applications surrounding the subterm contracted by
R
.
This means that
t=\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle
,
and there is a term in the list
[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]

of the form
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r

\ell^{\prime}
is also the external label of the type
\mathcal{A}
.
There are two subcases, depending on whether such term is

1.2.1

If
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle

Note that
\mathtt{C}_{1}

.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}

.
Then
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle)[\bar{s}_{1},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle)\,\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle)[\bar{s}_{1},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle)\,\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}

1.2.2

If
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r\neq\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle
.

Then
\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r

is either one of the terms in the list
\bar{s}_{1}

or one of the terms in the list
\bar{s}_{2}
.
Given any term, context,
or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let
\mathtt{X}^{*}
denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle\bar{s}_{2}]\}\!\!\}

and let
\mathtt{X}^{\dagger}
denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle\bar{s}_{2}]\}\!\!\}
.
Then
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle)\,[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{u}^{*}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{u}\rangle)\,[\bar{s}_{1},\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle\Sigma^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{s}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{\dagger}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{u}^{\dagger}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{\dagger}\langle r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}^{\dagger}\}\!\!\}\rangle}

2.

Under an abstraction,
\mathtt{C}=\lambda^{\ell}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
.

Straightforward by
i.h.
.

3.

Left of an application,
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\bar{u}
.

Let
\Delta
be the redex contracted by
R
, and let
\Delta^{\prime}
denote its contractum.
The step
R
is of the form
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\bar{u}\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\bar{u}
.
We consider three subcases, according to Creation (Lem.
13
), depending on whether

T
is created by
[crI]
,
[crII]
, or
[crIII]
.

3.1

[crI]

Then
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
is empty and
\Delta
has the following particular shape:

\Delta=(\lambda^{\ell}x.x^{\mathcal{A}})[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r]
.
The step
S
can be either internal to the subterm
r
,
or internal to one of the subterms in the list
\bar{u}
.
If
S
is internal to
r
, let
r^{\prime}
denote the term that results
from
r
after the step
S
.
Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.x^{\mathcal{A}})[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r]\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{r\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.x^{\mathcal{A}})[\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r^{\prime}]\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r^{\prime})\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{r^{\prime}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}}

Note that
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
, as required.
If, on the other hand,
S
is internal to
\bar{u}
, the situation is similar.

3.2

[crII]

Then
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
is empty and
\Delta
has the following particular shape:

\Delta=(\lambda^{\ell}x.\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{s}
.
The step
S
can be either internal to
r
, internal to
\bar{s}
,
or internal to
\bar{u}
.
If

is internal to
r
, let

denote the term that results
from

after the step

. Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r)\,\bar{s}\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\})\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{r\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell}x.\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r^{\prime})\,\bar{s}\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.r^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\})\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{r^{\prime}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}}

Note that
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
, as required.
If, on the other hand,
S
is internal to
\bar{s}
or
\bar{u}
, the situation is similar.

3.3

[crIII]

Recall that the step
R
is of the form

R:\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\,\bar{u}\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\,\bar{u}
.
Since the step
T
is created by
[crIII]
,
it does not take place at the root of
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[\Delta^{\prime}]\,\bar{u}
,
but rather it is internal to
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[\Delta^{\prime}]
.
We consider three subcases, depending on whether the step
S
takes place
at the root, to the left of the application or to the right of the application.

3.3.1

If

takes place at the root.

Then
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
is an abstraction,
so
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
cannot be empty,
i.e.
we have that
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
Moreover, since

is created by
[crIII]
,
we have that
\Delta
has the following specific shape:

\Delta=(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{r}\rangle)\,\bar{s}

where
\bar{s}=[\bar{s}_{1},\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p,\bar{s}_{2}]

and such that
\ell^{\prime\prime}
is the external label of
\mathcal{A}
.

Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let
\mathtt{X}^{*}
denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}\}\!\!\}

and let
\mathtt{X}^{\dagger}
denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{y:=\bar{u}\}\!\!\}
.
Note also that by the Substitution lemma (Lem.
10
) we have that

\mathtt{X}^{\dagger}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{s}^{\dagger}\}\!\!\}={\mathtt{X}^{*}}^{\dagger}
.
Then
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{r}\rangle)\,\bar{s}\rangle)\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p)\,\bar{r}^{*}\rangle\rangle)\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}\langle\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}\langle p\{\!\!\{z:=\bar{r}^{*}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\,\bar{u}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}}^{\dagger}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.\mathtt{C}_{2}^{\dagger}\langle x^{\mathcal{A}}\,\bar{r}^{\dagger}\rangle)\,\bar{s}^{\dagger}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}}^{\dagger}\langle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}}^{\dagger}\langle(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}z.p^{\dagger})\,{\bar{r}^{*}}{}^{\dagger}\rangle\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{C}^{\prime\prime}}^{\dagger}\langle{\mathtt{C}_{2}^{*}}^{\dagger}\langle p^{\dagger}\{\!\!\{z:={\bar{r}^{*}}{}^{\dagger}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle}

3.3.2

If
S
is internal to
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
.

Then it is straightforward by
i.h.
.

3.3.3

If
S
is internal to
\bar{u}
.

Then:

\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\,\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{p\,\bar{u}}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\,\bar{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\,\bar{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{p\,\bar{r}}

It is immediate to note in this case that
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
,
since the two-step sequences
RT
and
(R/S)(T/(S/R))

are both isomorphic to

\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}p
, only going below different contexts.

4.

Right of an application,
\mathtt{C}=u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime},\bar{r}_{2}]
.

Let
\Delta
be the redex contracted by
R
, and let
\Delta^{\prime}
denote its contractum.
The step
R
is of the form

u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]
.
We consider three subcases, depending on whether the step
S
takes place at the root,
to the left of the application, or to the right of the application.

4.1

If
S
takes place at the root.

Then
u
is an abstraction
u=\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.u^{\prime}
.
Moreover, since parameters are in 1–1 correspondence with arguments,
there is a free occurrence of
y
in
u^{\prime}
whose type is also the type of
the argument
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
. Let us write
u^{\prime}
as
u^{\prime}=\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle y^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle
,
where the hole of
\mathtt{C}_{1}
marks the position of such occurrence.
Given any term, context, or list of terms
\mathtt{X}
let
\mathtt{X}^{*}
denote
\mathtt{X}\{\!\!\{x:=\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2}\}\!\!\}
.
Then:

\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle y^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle)[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle y^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle)[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{(\lambda^{\ell^{\prime}}y.\mathtt{C}_{1}\langle y^{\mathcal{A}}\rangle)[\bar{r}_{1},p,\bar{r}_{2}]}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{\mathtt{C}_{1}^{*}\langle p\rangle}

So
R/S
creates
T/(S/R)
,
since the two-step sequences
RT
and
(R/S)(T/(S/R))

are both isomorphic to
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}p
,
only going below different contexts.

4.2

If
S
is internal to the left of the application.

Then:

\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},p,\bar{r}_{2}]}
\textstyle{u^{\prime}[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{u^{\prime}[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{u^{\prime}[\bar{r}_{1},p,\bar{r}_{2}]}

Then since
RT
and
(R/S)(T/(S/R))

are isomorphic, it is immediate to conclude,
similarly as in item
3.3.3
of this lemma.

4.3

If
S
is internal to the right of the application.

We consider two further subcases, depending on whether
S
is
internal to the argument
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
, or otherwise.

4.3.1

If
S
is internal to the argument
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
.

Then it is straightforward by
i.h.
.

4.3.2

If
S
is not internal to the argument
\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle
.

Then it is either internal to
\bar{r}_{1}
or to
\bar{r}_{2}
.
If it is internal to
\bar{r}_{1}
, then:

\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S/R}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}_{1},p,\bar{r}_{2}]}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}^{\prime}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R/S}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}^{\prime}_{1},\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\Delta^{\prime}\rangle,\bar{r}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T/(S/R)}
\textstyle{u[\bar{r}^{\prime}_{1},p,\bar{r}_{2}]}

Then since
RT
and
(R/S)(T/(S/R))

are isomorphic, it is immediate to conclude,
similarly as in item
3.3.3
of this lemma.

Lemma 15
(Full Stability)

Let
\rho,\sigma
be coinitial derivations
such that
\mathsf{labs}(\rho)\cap\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)=\varnothing
.
Let
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
be steps such that

T_{3}=T_{1}/(\sigma/\rho)=T_{2}/(\rho/\sigma)
.
Then there is a step
T_{0}
such that
T_{1}=T_{0}/\rho

and
T_{2}=T_{0}/\sigma
.

Proof

The proof of Full Stability (Lem.
15
)
is straightforward using
Basic Stability
(Lem.
14
).
Consider the diagram
formed by
\rho=R_{1}\ldots R_{n}

and
\sigma=S_{1}\ldots S_{m}
.
By Permutation (Prop.
2
)
the diagram can be tiled using squares, as in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}

there is no erasure or duplication. Note that for all
i\neq j
we have
that
R_{i}\neq S_{j}
,
since
\mathsf{labs}(\rho)
and
\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)
are disjoint.
It suffices to apply Basic Stability
n\times m
times, once for each tile.

0.A.5
Proof of Prop.
4
–
\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t)
is a lattice

Let
{[\rho]}
denote the permutation equivalence class of
a derivation
\rho
,
and let
\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t)=\{{[\rho]}\ |\ \mathsf{src}(\rho)=t\}

be the set of
\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}
-derivations
starting on
t
, modulo permutation equivalence.
We already know that
(\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t),\sqsubseteq)
is an upper
semilattice, where the order is given by

{[\rho]}\sqsubseteq{[\sigma]}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{\iff}\rho\sqsubseteq\sigma
,
the bottom is
\bot={[\epsilon]}

and the join is

{[\rho]}\sqcup{[\sigma]}={[\rho\sqcup\sigma]}
.

Note that
\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t)
has a top element:
given that
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
is strongly normalizing (Prop.
1
),
consider a derivation
\rho:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\texttt{{\#}}}}^{*}s
to normal form.
Then for any other derivation
\sigma:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\texttt{{\#}}}}^{*}u

we have that
\sigma/\rho=\epsilon
, so it suffices to take

\top={[\rho]}
.

To see that
\mathbb{D}^{\texttt{{\#}}}(t)
is a lattice for all
t\in\mathcal{T}^{\texttt{{\#}}}
,
we are left to show that any two coinitial derivations
\rho,\sigma

have a
meet

\rho\sqcap\sigma

with respect to the prefix order
\sqsubseteq
,
and define
{[\rho]}\sqcap{[\sigma]}\overset{\mathrm{def}}{=}{[\rho\sqcap\sigma]}
.
To this purpose, we prove various auxiliary results.

Definition 15

A step
R

belongs to
a coinitial derivation
\rho
,
written
R\in\rho
,
if and only if
some residual of
R
is contracted along
\rho
.
More precisely,

R\in\rho

if there exist
\rho_{1},R^{\prime},\rho_{2}
such that

\rho=\rho_{1}R\rho_{2}
and
R^{\prime}\in R/\rho_{1}
,
We write
R\not\in\rho

if it is not the case that
R\in\rho
.

Lemma 16
(Characterization of belonging)

Let
R
be a step and
\rho
a coinitial derivation
in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
.
Then the following are equivalent:

1.

R\in\rho
,

2.

R\sqsubseteq\rho
,

3.

\mathsf{lab}(R)\in\mathsf{labs}(\rho)
.

Note: the hypothesis that
R
and
\rho
are coinitial is crucial.
In particular,
(1)
and
(2)
by definition only hold when
R
and
\rho
are coinitial,
while
(3)
might hold even if
R
and
\rho
are not coinitial.

Proof

(1\Rightarrow 2)

Let
\rho=\rho_{1}S\sigma_{2}
where
S
is a residual of
R
.
Suppose moreover, without loss of generality,
that
\rho_{1}
is minimal,
i.e.
that
R\not\in\rho_{1}
.
Since in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
there is no duplication or erasure,

R/\rho_{1}
is a singleton, so
R/\rho_{1}=S
.
This means that
R/\rho_{1}S\rho_{2}=\varnothing
,
so indeed
R\sqsubseteq\rho_{1}S\rho_{2}
.

(2\Rightarrow 3)

By induction on
\rho
.
If
\rho
is empty, the implication is vacuously true,
so let
\rho=S\sigma

and consider two subcases, depending on whether
R=S
.
If
R=S
, then indeed the first step of
\rho=R\sigma

has the same label as
R
.
On the other hand, if
R\neq S
,
since in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
there is no duplication or erasure,
we have that
R/S=R^{\prime}
,
where
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})
.
Note that
R\sqsubseteq S\sigma

so
R/S=R^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma
.
By applying the
i.h.
we obtain that there must be a step in
\sigma

whose label is
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})
, and we are done.

(3\Rightarrow 1)

By induction on
\rho
.
If
\rho
is empty, the implication is vacuously true,
so let
\rho=S\sigma

and consider two subcases, depending on whether
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(S)
.
If
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(S)
then
R
and
S
must be the same step,
since terms are correct, which means that labels decorating lambdas are pairwise distinct.
Hence
R\in\rho=R\sigma
.
On the other hand, if
\mathsf{lab}(R)\neq\mathsf{lab}(S)
, then

R\neq S

so since in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
there is no duplication or erasure
we have that
R/S=R^{\prime}
,
where
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})
.
By hypothesis, there is a step in the derivation
\rho=S\sigma
whose label
is
\mathsf{lab}(R)
, and it is not
S
, so there must be at least one step in the
derivation
\sigma
whose label is
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})
.
By
i.h.

R^{\prime}\in\sigma

and then, since
R^{\prime}
is a residual of
R
,
we conclude that
R\in S\sigma
,
as required.

It is immediate to note that,
when composing derivations
\rho,\sigma
,
the set of labels of
\rho\sigma

results from the union of the labels of
\rho
and
\sigma
:

Remark 4

\mathsf{labs}(\rho\sigma)=\mathsf{labs}(\rho)\cup\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)

Indeed, a stronger results holds. Namely, the union is disjoint:

Lemma 17

\mathsf{labs}(\rho\sigma)=\mathsf{labs}(\rho)\uplus\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)

Proof

Let
\rho:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\texttt{{\#}}}}s
.
By induction on
\rho
we argue that the set of labels decorating the lambdas in
s

is disjoint from
\mathsf{labs}(\rho)
.
The base case is immediate, so let
\rho=T\tau
.
The step
T
is of the form:

T:t=\mathtt{C}\langle(\lambda^{\ell}x.u)\bar{r}\rangle\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}\mathtt{C}\langle u\{x:=\bar{r}\}\rangle
.
Hence the target of
T
has no lambdas decorated with the label
\ell
.
Moreover, the derivation
\tau
is of the form:

\tau:\mathtt{C}\langle u\{x:=\bar{r}\}\rangle\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\texttt{{\#}}}}s

and by
i.h.
the set of labels decorating the lambdas in
s

is disjoint from
\mathsf{labs}(\tau)
.
As a consequence,
the set of labels decorating the lambdas in
s

is disjoint from both
\mathsf{labs}(\tau)

and
\{\ell\}
.
This completes the proof.

As an easy consequence:

Corollary 3

If
|\rho|
denotes the length of
\rho
,
then

|\rho|=\#(\mathsf{labs}(\rho))
.

Lemma 18

Let
\rho
and
\sigma
be coinitial.
Then

\mathsf{labs}(\rho/\sigma)=\mathsf{labs}(\rho)\setminus\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)

Proof

First we claim that
\mathsf{labs}(\rho/R)=\mathsf{labs}(\rho)\setminus\{\mathsf{lab}(R)\}
.
By induction on
\rho
.
The base case is immediate, so let
\rho=S\sigma

and consider two subcases, depending on whether
R=S
.
If
R=S
,
then

\mathsf{labs}(\rho/R)=\mathsf{labs}(R\sigma/R)=\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)=\mathsf{labs}(R\sigma)\setminus\{\mathsf{lab}(R)\}
.
On the other hand if
R\neq S
,
then since
in
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
there is no duplication or erasure
we have that
R/S=R^{\prime}

where
\mathsf{lab}(R)=\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})

and, similarly,

S/R=S^{\prime}
,
where
\mathsf{lab}(S)=\mathsf{lab}(S^{\prime})
. Hence:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{labs}(\rho/R)&=&\mathsf{labs}(S\sigma/R)\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}((S/R)(\sigma/(R/S)))\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S/R)\cup\mathsf{labs}(\sigma/(R/S))\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S^{\prime})\cup\mathsf{labs}(\sigma/R^{\prime})\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S^{\prime})\cup(\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)\setminus\{\mathsf{lab}(R^{\prime})\}&\text{ by {i.h.} }\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S)\cup(\mathsf{labs}(\sigma)\setminus\{\mathsf{lab}(R)\})\\
