a term).
On the other hand,
R_{0}
is coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
, so in particular
it is not
t^{\prime}
-garbage.
This means that the pattern of the redex
R_{0}
cannot occur inside the argument
u

of the redex
S
.
So
S
does not erase or duplicate
R
,

i.e.

R_{0}
has exactly one residual
R_{1}\in R_{0}/S
.

Now we have that
R_{0}/S\tau=R_{1}/\tau
.
We are left to show that
\#(R_{0}/S\tau)\leq 1
.
Let us show that we may apply the
i.h.
on
R_{1}
.
More precisely, observe that
\tau\sqsubseteq\rho/S
since
S\tau\sqsubseteq\rho
.
To apply the
i.h.
it suffices to show that
R_{1}
is coarse for
(\rho/S,t^{\prime}/S)
.
Indeed, we may check the two conditions
for the definition that
R_{1}
is coarse for
(\rho/S,t^{\prime}/S)
.

2.1

Firstly,
R_{1}=R_{0}/S\sqsubseteq\rho/S
holds, as a consequence of the fact that
R_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho
.

2.2

Secondly, we may check that
R_{1}
is not
(t^{\prime}/S)
-garbage.
To see this,
i.e.
that
R_{1}/(t^{\prime}/S)
is non-empty,
we check that
\mathsf{labs}(R_{1}/(t^{\prime}/S))
is non-empty.

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{labs}(R_{1}/(t^{\prime}/S))&=&\mathsf{labs}((R_{0}/S)/(t^{\prime}/S))&\text{ by definition of $R_{1}$}\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}((R_{0}/t^{\prime})/(S/t^{\prime}))&\text{ by Lem.~\ref{lemma:generalized_cube_lemma}}\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(R_{0}/t^{\prime})\setminus\mathsf{labs}(S/t^{\prime})&\text{ by Lem.~\ref{lemma:names_after_projection_along_a_step}}\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(R_{0}/t^{\prime})\end{array}

For the last step, note that
\mathsf{labs}(R_{0}/t^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{labs}(S/t^{\prime})
are disjoint,
since
R_{0}\neq S
.

Applying the
i.h.
, we obtain that
\#(R_{0}/S\tau)=\#(R_{1}/\tau)\leq 1
.

Lemma 39

Let
R
be a step, let
\rho
a coinitial derivation,
and let
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}\mathsf{src}(R)
.
Suppose that
R
is not
t^{\prime}
-garbage,
and that
R/\rho_{1}
is a singleton for every prefix
\rho_{1}\sqsubseteq\rho
.
Then
R/\rho
is not
(t^{\prime}/\rho)
-garbage.

Proof

By induction on
\rho
.
The base case, when
\rho=\epsilon
, is immediate
since we know that
R
is not garbage.
For the inductive step, suppose that
\rho=S\sigma
.
We know that
R/S
is a singleton,
so let
R_{1}=R/S
.
Note that

R_{1}/(t^{\prime}/S)=(R/S)/(t^{\prime}/S)=(R/t^{\prime})/(S/t^{\prime})
by Lem.
4
.
We know that
R/t^{\prime}
is non-empty, because
R
is not garbage.
Moreover,
\mathsf{labs}(R/t^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{labs}(S/t^{\prime})
are disjoint
since
R\neq S
.
So
\#\mathsf{labs}((R/t^{\prime})/(S/t^{\prime}))=\mathsf{labs}(R/t^{\prime})
by Lem.
18
.
This means that the set
R_{1}/(t^{\prime}/S)
is non-empty,
so
R_{1}
is not
(t^{\prime}/S)
-garbage .
By
i.h.
we obtain that
R_{1}/\sigma
is not
((t^{\prime}/S)/\sigma)
-garbage,
which means that

(R/S\sigma)/(t^{\prime}/S\sigma)\neq\varnothing
,

i.e.
that
R/S\sigma
is not garbage, as required.
To be able to apply the
i.h.
, observe that if
\sigma_{1}
is a prefix of
\sigma
,
then
S\sigma_{1}
is a prefix of
\rho
,
so the fact that
R
has a single residual after
S\sigma_{1}

implies that the step
R_{1}=R/S
has a single residual after
\sigma_{1}
.

Proposition 14
(Characterization of garbage-free derivations)

Let
\rho:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\beta}}s
and
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}t
.
The following are equivalent:

1.

\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free.

2.

\rho\equiv\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

3.

\rho\equiv\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
for some derivation
\sigma
.

Proof

Let us prove
1\implies 2\implies 3\implies 1
:

•

(1\implies 2)

Suppose that
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free,
and let us show that
\rho\equiv\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}

by induction on the length of
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

If there are no coarse steps for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
,
By Prop.
13
, any derivation with no coarse steps is garbage.
So
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage.
Since
\rho
is garbage-free, this means that
\rho=\epsilon
.
Hence
\rho=\epsilon=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
, as required.

If there exists a coarse step for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
, let
R_{0}
be the leftmost such step.
Note that
\rho\equiv R_{0}(\rho/R_{0})
since
R_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho
.
Moreover, we claim that
\rho/R_{0}
is
(t^{\prime}/R_{0})
-garbage-free.
Let
\sigma\sqsubseteq\rho/R_{0}
such that
(\rho/R_{0})/\sigma
is garbage
with respect to the term
(t^{\prime}/R_{0})/\sigma=t^{\prime}/R_{0}\sigma
,
and let us show that
(\rho/R_{0})/\sigma
is empty.
Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}R_{0}\sigma&\sqsubseteq&R_{0}(\rho/R_{0})&\text{since $\sigma\sqsubseteq\rho/R_{0}$}\\
&\equiv&\rho&\text{as already noted}\\
\end{array}

Moreover, we know that the derivation
\rho/R_{0}\sigma=(\rho/R_{0})/\sigma
is

(t^{\prime}/R_{0}\sigma)
-garbage.
So, given that
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free, we conclude that

\rho/R_{0}\sigma=\epsilon
, that is
(\rho/R_{0})/\sigma=\epsilon
,
which completes the proof of the claim that
\rho/R_{0}
is
(t^{\prime}/R_{0})
-garbage-free.
We conclude as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho&\equiv&R_{0}(\rho/R_{0})&\text{as already noted}\\
&\equiv&R_{0}((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))&\text{by {i.h.} since $\rho/R_{0}$ is $(t^{\prime}/R_{0})$-garbage-free}\\
&\equiv&\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}&\text{by definition of sieving}\\
\end{array}

•

(2\implies 3)
Obvious, taking
\sigma:=\rho
.

•

(3\implies 1)

Let
\rho\equiv\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
Let us show that
\rho
is garbage-free by induction on the length of
\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

If there are no coarse steps for
(\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
then
\rho\equiv\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\epsilon
, which means that
\rho=\epsilon
.
Observe that the empty derivation is trivially garbage-free.

If there exists a coarse step for
(\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
let
R_{0}
be the leftmost such step.
Then
\rho\equiv\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=R_{0}((\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))
.
To see that
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free,
let
\tau\sqsubseteq\rho
such that
\rho/\tau
is garbage,
and let us show that
\rho/\tau
is empty.
We know that
\rho/\tau
is of the following form (modulo permutation equivalence):

\rho/\tau\equiv\frac{R_{0}((\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))}{\tau}=\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\tau}\right)\left(\frac{(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})}{\tau/R_{0}}\right)

That is, we know that the following derivation is
(t^{\prime}/\tau)
-garbage,
and it suffices to show that it is empty:

\left(\frac{R_{0}}{\tau}\right)\left(\frac{(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})}{\tau/R_{0}}\right)

Recall that, in general,
AB
is garbage if and only if
A
and
B
are garbage (Prop.
9
).
Similarly,
AB
is empty if and only if
A
and
B
are empty.
So it suffices to prove the two following implications:

\begin{array}[]{clll}{\bf(A)}&\text{ If }&R_{0}/\tau\text{ is garbage,}&\text{ then it is empty.}\\
{\bf(B)}&\text{ If }&((\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))/(\tau/R_{0})\text{ is garbage,}&\text{ then it is empty.}\end{array}

Let us check that each implication holds:

–

(A)

Suppose that
R_{0}/\tau
is
(t^{\prime}/\tau)
-garbage,
and let us show that
R_{0}/\tau
is empty.
Knowing that the derivation
R_{0}/\tau
is garbage means that

(R_{0}/\tau)/(t^{\prime}/\tau)=\varnothing
.
Since
R_{0}
is the leftmost step coarse for
(\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
by Lem.
38

we have that
\#(R_{0}/\tau)\leq 1
.
If
R_{0}/\tau
is empty we are done, since this is what we wanted to prove.

The remaining possibility is that
R_{0}/\tau
be a singleton.
We argue that this case is impossible.
Note that for every prefix
\tau_{1}\sqsubseteq\tau
,
the set
R_{0}/\tau_{1}
is also a singleton,
since otherwise it would be empty, as a consequence of Lem.
38
.
So we may apply Lem.
39
and conclude that,
since
R_{0}
is not
t^{\prime}
-garbage
then
R_{0}/\tau
is not
(t^{\prime}/\tau)
-garbage.
This contradicts the hypothesis.

–

(B)

Suppose that
((\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))/(\tau/R_{0})
is garbage
with respect to the term
(t^{\prime}/R_{0})/(\tau/R_{0})
, and let us show that it is empty.
Since
(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})

is a shorter derivation than
\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
,
we may apply the
i.h.
we obtain that

(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})
is
(t^{\prime}/R_{0})
-garbage-free.
Moreover, the following holds:

\tau/R_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho/R_{0}\equiv(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})

So, by definition of
(\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0})

being garbage-free,
the fact that
the derivation

((\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))/(\tau/R_{0})
is garbage
implies that it is empty, as required.

Lemma 40
(The projection after a sieve is garbage)

Let
\rho:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\beta}}s
and
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}t
.
Then
\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is
(t^{\prime}/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))
-garbage.

Proof

By induction on the length of
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

If there are no coarse steps for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
,
then
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\epsilon
.
Moreover, by Prop.
13
, a derivation with no
coarse steps is garbage. So
\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})=\rho

is garbage, as required.

If there exists a coarse step for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
,
let
R_{0}
be the leftmost such step,
and let
\sigma=\rho/R_{0}
and
s^{\prime}=t^{\prime}/R_{0}
.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})&=&\rho/(R_{0}((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))&\text{ by definition}\\
&=&(\rho/R_{0})/((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))\\
&=&\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow s^{\prime})\\
\end{array}

By
i.h.
,
\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow s^{\prime})
is
(s^{\prime}/(\sigma\Downarrow s^{\prime}))
-garbage.
That is:

\frac{\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow s^{\prime})}{s^{\prime}/(\sigma\Downarrow s^{\prime})}=\epsilon

Unfolding the definitions of
\sigma
and
s^{\prime}
we have
that:

\frac{(\rho/R_{0})/((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))}{(t^{\prime}/R_{0})/((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))}=\epsilon

Equivalently:

\frac{\rho/R_{0}((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))}{t^{\prime}/R_{0}((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))}=\epsilon

Finally, by definition of sieve,

\frac{\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})}{t^{\prime}/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})}=\epsilon

which means that
\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is
(t^{\prime}/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))
-garbage,
as required.

The proof of Prop.
10
is a consequence of the preceding results, namely:

1.

Proof that
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free and
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho
.

Note that
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free by Prop.
14
.
Moreover,
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho
by Lem.
35
.

2.

Proof that
\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is
(t^{\prime}/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))
-garbage.

This is precisely Lem.
40
.

3.

Proof that
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage if and only if
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\epsilon
.

An immediate consequence of Prop.
13
.

4.

Proof that
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free if and only if
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\rho
.

An immediate consequence of Prop.
14
.

0.A.14
Proof of Prop.
11
– Semilattices of garbage-free and garbage derivations

To prove Prop.
11
we need a few
auxiliary lemmas:

Lemma 41

Let
\rho\equiv\sigma
. Then
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

Proof

Observe that, given two permutation equivalent derivations
\rho
and
\sigma
,
a step
R
is coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
if and only if
R
is coarse for
(\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
since

(R\sqsubseteq\rho)\iff(R/\rho=\varnothing)\iff(R/\sigma=\varnothing)\iff(R\sqsubseteq\sigma)
.
Using this observation, the proof is straightforward by induction on the length of
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

Lemma 42
(Sieving trailing garbage)

Let
\rho
and
\sigma
be composable derivations, and let
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}\mathsf{src}(\rho)
.
If
\sigma
is
(t^{\prime}/\rho)
-garbage, then
\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.

Proof

By induction on the length of
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
If there are no coarse steps for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
,
then by Prop.
13
,
the derivation
\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage,
so
\rho\sigma
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage by Prop.
9
.
Resorting to Prop.
13

we obtain that
\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\epsilon=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
,
as required.

If there exists a coarse step for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
,
let
R_{0}
be the leftmost such step.
Then since
R_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho
also
R_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma
,
so
R_{0}
is a coarse step for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
.
In particular, since there exists at least one coarse step for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
let
S_{0}
be the leftmost such step.
We argue that
R_{0}=S_{0}
.
We consider two cases, depending on whether
S_{0}
is coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
:

1.

If
S_{0}
is coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
.

Then
R_{0}
and
S_{0}
are both simultaneously
coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
and for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
.
Note that
R_{0}
cannot be to the left of
S_{0}
,
since then
S_{0}
would not be the leftmost coarse step for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
.
Symmetrically,
S_{0}
cannot be to the left of
R_{0}
,
since then
R_{0}
would not be the leftmost coarse step for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
.
Hence
R_{0}=S_{0}
as claimed.

2.

If
S_{0}
is not coarse for
(\rho,t^{\prime})
.

We argue that this case is impossible.
Note that
S_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma
but it is not the case that
S_{0}\sqsubseteq\rho
,
so
S_{0}/\rho
is not empty.
Note also that
S_{0}/\rho\sqsubseteq\sigma
,
so by Coro.
2

we have that
(S_{0}/\rho)/(t^{\prime}/\rho)\sqsubseteq\sigma/(t^{\prime}/\rho)
.
Moreover,
\sigma/(t^{\prime}/\rho)=\epsilon
is empty because
\sigma
is
(t^{\prime}/\rho)
-garbage.
This means that
(S_{0}/\rho)/(t^{\prime}/\rho)=\epsilon
.
Then we have the following chain of equalities:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\varnothing&=&\mathsf{labs}((S_{0}/\rho)/(t^{\prime}/\rho))\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}((S_{0}/t^{\prime})/(\rho/t^{\prime}))&\text{by Coro.~\ref{coro:permutation_equivalence_in_terms_of_names} and Lem.~\ref{lemma:generalized_cube_lemma}}\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S_{0}/t^{\prime})\setminus\mathsf{labs}(\rho/t^{\prime})&\text{by Lem.~\ref{lemma:names_after_projection_along_a_step}}\\
&=&\mathsf{labs}(S_{0}/t^{\prime})\end{array}

To justify the last step, start by noting that no residual of
S_{0}
is contracted along the derivation
\rho
.
Indeed, if
S_{0}
had a residual, then
\rho=\rho_{1}S_{1}\rho_{2}
where
S_{1}\in S_{0}/\rho
.
But recall that
S_{0}
is the leftmost coarse step for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
and

\rho_{1}\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma
, so it has at most one residual (Lem.
38
).
This means that
S_{0}/\rho_{1}=S_{1}
, so
S_{0}/\rho=\varnothing
, which is a contradiction.
Given that no residuals of
S_{0}
are contracted along the derivation
\rho
,
we have that the sets
\mathsf{labs}(S_{0}/t^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{labs}(\rho/t^{\prime})
are disjoint
which justifies the last step.

According to the chain of equalities above, we have that
\mathsf{labs}(S_{0}/t^{\prime})=\varnothing
.
This means that
S_{0}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage.
This in turn contradicts the fact that
S_{0}
is coarse for
(\rho\sigma,t^{\prime})
,
confirming that this case is impossible.

We have just claimed that
R_{0}=S_{0}
. Then we conclude as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}&=&R_{0}((\rho\sigma/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))\\
&=&R_{0}(((\rho/R_{0})(\sigma/(R_{0}/\rho)))\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))\\
&=&R_{0}((\rho/R_{0})\Downarrow(t^{\prime}/R_{0}))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{by {i.h.}, as $\sigma/(R_{0}/\rho)$ is garbage by Prop.~\ref{prop:properties_of_garbage}}\\
&=&\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

Let us check the two items of Prop.
11
:

1.

The set
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
forms a finite lattice.

Let us check all the conditions:

1.1

Partial order.

First let us show that
\unlhd
is a partial order.

1.1.1

Reflexivity.

It is immediate that
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\rho]}
holds since
\rho/\rho=\epsilon
is garbage.

1.1.2

Antisymmetry.

Let
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}\unlhd{[\rho]}
.
This means that
\rho/\sigma
and
\sigma/\rho
are garbage.
Then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho&\equiv&\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}&\text{ since $\rho$ is garbage-free, by Prop.~\ref{prop:characterization_of_garbage_free_derivations}}\\
&\equiv&\rho(\sigma/\rho)\Downarrow t^{\prime}&\text{ since $\sigma/\rho$ is garbage, by Lem.~\ref{lemma:sieving_trailing_garbage}}\\
&\equiv&\sigma(\rho/\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}&\text{ since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$ in general, using Lem.~\ref{lemma:sieving_is_compatible_with_permutation_equivalence}}\\
&\equiv&\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}&\text{ since $\rho/\sigma$ is garbage, by Lem.~\ref{lemma:sieving_trailing_garbage}}\\
&\equiv&\sigma&\text{ since $\sigma$ is garbage-free, by Prop.~\ref{prop:characterization_of_garbage_free_derivations}}\\
\end{array}

Since
\rho\equiv\sigma
we conclude that
{[\rho]}={[\sigma]}
,
as required.

1.1.3

Transitivity.

Let
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}\unlhd{[\tau]}

and let us show that
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\tau]}
.
Note that
\rho/\sigma
and
\sigma/\tau
are garbage.
By the fact that the projection of garbage is garbage (Prop.
9
)
the derivation
(\rho/\sigma)/(\sigma/\tau)
is garbage.
The composition of garbage is also garbage (Prop.
9
),
so
(\sigma/\tau)((\rho/\sigma)/(\sigma/\tau))
is garbage.
In general the following holds:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho/\tau&\sqsubseteq&(\rho/\tau)((\sigma/\tau)/(\rho/\tau))&\text{ since $A\sqsubseteq AB$ in general}\\
&\equiv&(\sigma/\tau)((\rho/\tau)/(\sigma/\tau))&\text{ since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$ in general}\\
&\equiv&(\sigma/\tau)((\rho/\sigma)/(\tau/\sigma))&\text{ since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$ in general}\\
\end{array}

So since any prefix of a garbage derivation is garbage (Prop.
9
)
we conclude that
\rho/\tau
is garbage.
This means that
[\rho]\unlhd[\tau]
, as required.

1.2

Finite.

Let us check that
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is a finite lattice,
i.e.
that it has a finite number of elements.
Recall that
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is the set of equivalence classes
{[\rho]}
where
\rho

is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free.

On one hand, recall that the
\lambda^{\texttt{{\#}}}
-calculus is finitely branching and strongly normalizing (Prop.
1
).
So by König’s lemma the set of
\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}
-derivations starting from
t^{\prime}

is bounded in length. More precisely, there is a constant
M
such that
for any
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{\texttt{{\#}}}
and any derivation
\rho:t^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\texttt{{\#}}}}^{*}s^{\prime}

we have that the length
|\rho|
is bounded by
M
.

On the other hand, let
F=\{\rho\ |\ \rho\text{ is $t^{\prime}$-garbage-free and }\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\rho\}
.
Consider the mapping
\varphi:\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)\to F

given by
{[\rho]}\mapsto\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
, and note that:

•

\varphi
does not depend on the choice of representative,
that is, if
{[\rho]}={[\sigma]}
then
\varphi({[\rho]})=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\varphi({[\sigma]})
.
This is a consequence of Lem.
41
.

•

\varphi
is a well-defined function, that is
\varphi({[\rho]})\in F
.
This is because
\varphi({[\rho]})=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free by Prop.
10
.
Moreover, we have that
\varphi({[\rho]})\Downarrow t^{\prime}=({[\rho]}\Downarrow t^{\prime})\Downarrow t^{\prime}={[\rho]}\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\varphi({[\rho]})
,
which is also a consequence of Lem.
41
,
and the fact that
{[\rho]}\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\rho
(Prop.
10
).

•

\varphi
is injective. Indeed, suppose that
\varphi({[\rho]})=\varphi({[\sigma]})
,
that is,
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
Then
\rho\equiv\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\sigma

by Prop.
10
since
\rho
and
\sigma
are
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free.
Hence
{[\rho]}={[\sigma]}
.

Since
\varphi:\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)\to F
is injective, it suffices to show that
F
is finite
to conclude that
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is finite.
Recall that the
\lambda
-calculus is finitely branching, so the number of derivations of a certain length
is finite. To show that
F
is finite, it suffices to show that the length of derivations in
F
is
bounded by some constant.
Let
\rho
be a derivation in
F
. We have that
\rho=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
By construction of the sieve, none of the steps of
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
are garbage.
That is
\rho=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}=R_{1}\ldots R_{n}
where for all
i
we have that

R_{i}/(t^{\prime}/R_{1}\ldots R_{i-1})\neq\varnothing
.
So we have that the length of
\rho/t^{\prime}
is greater than the length of
\rho

for all
\rho\in F
:

|\rho/t^{\prime}|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\underbrace{R/(t^{\prime}/R_{1}\ldots R_{i-1})}_{\neq\varnothing}|\geq n=|\rho|

As a consequence given any
\mathrel{\to_{\beta}}
-derivation
\rho\in F
we have that
|\rho|\leq|\rho/t^{\prime}|\leq M
.
This concludes the proof that
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is finite.

1.3

Bottom element.

As the bottom element take
\bot_{(\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t))}:={[\epsilon]}
.
Observe that this is well-defined since
\epsilon
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free.
Moreover, let us show that
\bot_{(\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t))}
is the least element.
Let
[\rho]
be an arbitrary element of
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)

and let us check that
\bot_{(\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t))}\unlhd[\rho]
.
This is immediate since,
by definition,
\bot_{(\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t))}\unlhd[\rho]

if and only if
\epsilon/\rho
is garbage.
But
\epsilon/\rho=\epsilon
is trivially garbage.

1.4

Join.

Let
[\rho],[\sigma]
be arbitrary elements of
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
,
and let us check that
[\rho]\triangledown[\sigma]
is the join.
First observe that
[\rho]\triangledown[\sigma]
is well-defined

i.e.
that
(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free,
which is an immediate consequence of Prop.
14
.
Moreover, it is indeed the least upper bound of
\{[\rho],[\sigma]\}
:

1.4.1

Upper bound.

Let us show that
[\rho]\unlhd[\rho]\triangledown[\sigma]
; the proof for
[\sigma]
is symmetrical.
We must show that
\rho/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is garbage.
Note that
\rho\sqsubseteq\rho\sqcup\sigma
,
so in particular
\rho/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})\sqsubseteq(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})
.
Given that any prefix of a garbage derivation is garbage (Prop.
9
),
it suffices to show that
(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is garbage.
This is a straightforward consequence of the fact that projecting after a sieve is garbage (Lem.
40
).

1.4.2

Least upper bound.

Let
[\rho],[\sigma]\unlhd[\tau]
,
and let us show that
[\rho]\triangledown[\sigma]\unlhd[\tau]
.
We know that
\rho/\tau
and
\sigma/\tau
are garbage,
and we are to show that
((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})/\tau

is garbage.
Note that
(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho\sqcup\sigma

as a consequence of the fact that the sieve is a prefix (Lem.
35
).
So in particular
((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})/\tau\sqsubseteq(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/\tau
.
Given that any prefix of a garbage derivation is garbage (Prop.
9
),
it suffices to show that
(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/\tau
is garbage.
But
(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/\tau\equiv\rho/\tau\sqcup\sigma/\tau

so we conclude by the fact that the join of garbage is garbage (Prop.
9
).

1.5

Top element.

Since
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is finite,
its elements are
\{{[\tau_{1}]},\ldots,{[\tau_{n}]}\}
.
It suffices to take
\top:={[\tau_{1}]}\triangledown\ldots\triangledown{[\tau_{n}]}

as the top element.

1.6

Meet.

Let
{[\rho]},{[\sigma]}\in\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
.
Note that the set
L=\{{[\tau]}\in\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)\ |\ {[\tau]}\unlhd{[\rho]}\text{ and }{[\tau]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}\}

is finite because, as we have already proved, the set
\mathbb{F}(t^{\prime},t)
is itself finite.
Then
L=\{{[\tau_{1}]},\ldots,{[\tau_{n}]}\}
.
Take
{[\rho]}\!\vartriangle\!{[\sigma]}:={[\tau_{1}]}\triangledown\ldots\triangledown{[\tau_{n}]}
.
It is straightforward to show that
{[\rho]}\!\vartriangle\!{[\sigma]}

thus defined is the greatest lower bound for
\{{[\rho]},{[\sigma]}\}
.

2.

The set
\mathbb{G}(t^{\prime},t)
forms an upper semilattice.

The semilattice structure of
\mathbb{G}(t^{\prime},t)
is inherited from
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
.
More precisely,
{[\rho]}\sqsubseteq{[\sigma]}
is declared to hold if
\rho\sqsubseteq\sigma
,
the bottom element is
{[\epsilon]}
, and the join is

{[\rho]}\sqcup{[\sigma]}={[\rho\sqcup\sigma]}
.
Let us check that this is an upper semilattice:

2.1

Partial order.

The relation
(\sqsubseteq)
is a partial order on
\mathbb{G}(t^{\prime},t)
because it is already a partial order in
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
.

2.2

Bottom element.

It suffices to note that the empty derivation
\epsilon:t\mathrel{\to_{\beta}}^{*}t

is
t^{\prime}
-garbage, so
{[\epsilon]}\in\mathbb{G}(t^{\prime},t)

is the least element.

2.3

Join.

By Prop.
9
we know that if
\rho
and
\sigma

are
t^{\prime}
-garbage then
\rho\sqcup\sigma
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage,
so
{[\rho]}\sqcup{[\sigma]}
is indeed the least upper bound
of
\{{[\rho]},{[\sigma]}\}
.

0.A.15
Proof of Thm.
5.1
– Factorization

We need a few auxiliary lemmas:

Lemma 43
(Garbage-free/garbage decomposition)

Let
\rho:t\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow_{\beta}}s
and
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}t
.
Then there exist
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
such that:

1.

\rho\equiv\rho_{1}\rho_{2}

2.

\rho_{1}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free,

3.

\rho_{2}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage.

Moreover,
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
are unique modulo permutation equivalence,
and we have that

\rho_{1}\equiv\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
and
\rho_{2}\equiv\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
.

Proof

Let us prove that said decomposition exists and that it is unique:

•

Existence.

Take
\rho_{1}:=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}
and
\rho_{2}:=\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
.
Then we may check the three conditions in the statement:

1.

Recall that
\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho
holds by Lem.
35
, so:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\rho_{1}\rho_{2}&=&(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})(\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))\\
&\equiv&\rho((\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})/\rho)&\text{ since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$ holds in general}\\
&\equiv&\rho&\text{ since $\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho$}\end{array}

as required.

2.

The derivation
\rho_{1}=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}

is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free.
This is as an immediate consequence of Prop.
14
,
namely the result of sieving is always garbage-free.

3.

The derivation
\rho_{2}=\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is garbage.
This is an immediate consequence of Lem.
40
,
namely projection after a sieve is always garbage.

•

Uniqueness, modulo permutation equivalence.

Let
\rho\equiv\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}

where
\sigma_{1}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free,
and
\sigma_{2}
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage.
Then we argue that
\sigma_{1}\equiv\rho_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}\equiv\rho_{2}
.

Since
\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\equiv\rho\equiv\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}

and sieving is compatible with permutation equivalence (Lem.
41
)
we have that
\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
By Lem.
42
, we know that trailing garbage does not affect sieving,
hence
\rho_{1}\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\sigma_{1}\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
\rho_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}
are garbage-free, which
by Prop.
14
means
that
\rho_{1}\equiv\sigma_{1}
.
This means that
\rho_{1}
is unique, modulo permutation equivalence.
Finally, since
\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\equiv\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}
and
\rho_{1}\equiv\sigma_{1}
,
we have that
\rho_{1}\rho_{2}/\rho_{1}\equiv\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}/\sigma_{1}
,
that is
\rho_{2}\equiv\sigma_{2}
.
This means that
\rho_{2}
is unique, modulo permutation equivalence.

Lemma 44

Let
t^{\prime}\mathrel{\ltimes}\mathsf{src}(\rho)=\mathsf{src}(\sigma)
.
Then
{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}={[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\triangledown{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}
.

Proof

Let:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\alpha&:=&\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})\\
\beta&:=&\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})\\
\gamma&:=&(\alpha/((\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})))\sqcup(\beta/((\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})))\\
\end{array}

Note that
\alpha
and
\beta
are garbage by Lem.
40

and hence
\gamma
is also garbage, as a consequence of
the facts that the join of garbage is garbage
and that the projection of garbage is garbage (Prop.
9
).
Remark that, in general,
AB\sqcup CD\equiv(A\sqcup C)(B/(C/A)\sqcup D/(A/C))
.
Then the statement of this lemma is a consequence of the following chain of equalities:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}&=&{[((\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})\alpha\sqcup(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})\beta)\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}&\text{by Lem.~\ref{lemma:garbage_free_garbage_decomposition}}\\
&=&{[((\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})\sqcup(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}))\gamma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}&\text{by the previous remark}\\
&=&{[((\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})\sqcup(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}))\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}&\text{by~Lem.~\ref{lemma:sieving_trailing_garbage}}\\
&=&{[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\triangledown{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}&\text{by definition of $\triangledown$}\end{array}

To prove Thm.
5.1
,
let us check that
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
is well-defined,
that it is an upper semilattice,
and finally that
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)\simeq\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}

are isomorphic as upper semilattices.

1.

The Grothendieck construction
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
is well-defined.

This amounts to checking that
\mathcal{G}
is indeed a lax 2-functor:

1.1

The mapping
\mathcal{G}
is well-defined on objects.

Note that
\mathcal{G}({[\rho]})=\mathbb{G}(t^{\prime}/\rho,\mathsf{tgt}(\rho))
, which is a poset.
Moreover, the choice of the representative
\rho
of the equivalence class
{[\rho]}

does not matter,
since if
\rho
and
\sigma
are permutation equivalent derivations,
then
t^{\prime}/\rho=t^{\prime}/\sigma
(by Prop.
8
)
and
\mathsf{tgt}(\rho)=\mathsf{tgt}(\sigma)
.

1.2

The mapping
\mathcal{G}
is well-defined on morphisms.

Let us check that given
{[\rho]},{[\sigma]}\in\mathcal{F}

such that
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}

then

\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}):\mathcal{G}({[\rho]})\to\mathcal{G}({[\sigma]})
is a morphism of posets,
i.e.
a monotonic function.

First, we can see that it is well-defined,
since if
{[\alpha]}\in\mathcal{G}([\rho])

then the image
\mathcal{G}({[\rho]})({[\alpha]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})={[\rho\alpha/\sigma]}

is an element of
\mathcal{G}([\sigma])
,
since

\rho\alpha/\sigma=(\rho/\sigma)(\alpha/(\sigma/\rho))

is garbage, as it is the composition of garbage derivations (Prop.
9
):
the derivation
\rho/\sigma
is garbage since
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}
(by definition),
and the derivation
\alpha/(\sigma/\rho)
is garbage since
\alpha
is garbage (Prop.
9
).
Moreover, the choice of representative does not matter,
since if
\rho_{1}\equiv\rho_{2}
and
\sigma_{1}\equiv\sigma_{2}
and
\alpha_{1}\equiv\alpha_{2}

then
\rho_{1}\alpha_{1}/\sigma_{1}\equiv\rho_{2}\alpha_{2}/\sigma_{2}
.

We are left to verify that
\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})
is monotonic.
Let
{[\alpha]},{[\beta]}\in\mathcal{G}([\rho])

such that
{[\alpha]}\sqsubseteq{[\beta]}
, and let us show that

\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})({[\alpha]})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{G}([\rho]\hookrightarrow[\sigma])({[\beta]})
.
Indeed,
\alpha\sqsubseteq\beta
, so

\rho\alpha/\sigma=(\rho/\sigma)(\alpha/(\sigma/\rho))\sqsubseteq(\rho/\sigma)(\beta/(\sigma/\rho))=\rho\beta/\sigma
.

1.3

Identity.

Let
{[\rho]}\in\mathcal{F}
.
Let us check that
\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho]})=\mathsf{id}_{\mathcal{G}({[\rho]})}

is the identity morphism.
Indeed, if
{[\alpha]}\in\mathcal{G}({[\rho]})
,
then
\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho]})({[\alpha]})={[\rho\alpha/\rho]}={[\alpha]}
.

1.4

Composition.

Let
{[\rho]},{[\sigma]},{[\tau]}\in\mathcal{F}

such that
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\sigma]}\unlhd{[\tau]}
.
Let us check that there is a 2-cell

\mathcal{G}(({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}))\sqsubseteq\mathcal{G}({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})
.
Note that

({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}):{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\tau]}

is a morphism in the upper semilattice
\mathcal{F}
seen as a category.
Moreover, since it is a semilattice, there a unique morphism
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\tau]}
,
namely
{[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]}
, so we have that

({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})={[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]}
.
Now if
{[\alpha]}\in\mathcal{G}{{[\rho]}}
, then:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{G}(({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}))({[\alpha]})&=&\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})({[\alpha]})\\
&=&\rho\alpha/\tau\\
&\sqsubseteq&(\rho\alpha/\tau)((\sigma/\rho\alpha)/(\tau/\rho\alpha))\\
&=&\rho\alpha(\sigma/\rho\alpha)/\tau\\
&\equiv&\sigma(\rho\alpha/\sigma)/\tau\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$}\\
&=&\mathcal{G}({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})(\rho\alpha/\sigma)\\
&=&(\mathcal{G}({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}))({[\alpha]})\end{array}

so

\mathcal{G}(({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]}))\sqsubseteq\mathcal{G}({[\sigma]}\hookrightarrow{[\tau]})\circ\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma]})

as required.

2.

The Grothendieck construction
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
is an upper semilattice.

2.1

Partial order.

Recall that
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
is always poset
with the order given by
(a,b)\leq(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
if and only if

a\unlhd a^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}(a\hookrightarrow a^{\prime})(b)\sqsubseteq b^{\prime}
.

2.2

Bottom element.

We argue that
(\bot_{\mathcal{F}},\bot_{\mathcal{G}(\bot_{\mathcal{F}})})
is the bottom
element. Let
({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})\in\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
.
Then clearly
\bot_{\mathcal{F}}\unlhd{[\rho]}
. Moreover,

\mathcal{G}([\bot_{\mathcal{F}}]\hookrightarrow[\rho])(\bot_{\mathcal{G}})=[\epsilon/\rho]=[\epsilon]\sqsubseteq{[\sigma]}
.

2.3

Join.

Let us show that
(a,b)\lor(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=(a\triangledown a^{\prime},\mathcal{G}(a\hookrightarrow(a\triangledown a^{\prime}))(b)\sqcup\mathcal{G}(a^{\prime}\hookrightarrow(a\triangledown a^{\prime}))(b^{\prime}))
.
is the least upper bound of
\{(a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\}
.

2.3.1

Upper bound.

Let us show that
(a,b)\leq(a,b)\lor(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
.
Recall that
(a,b)\lor(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=(a\triangledown a,\mathcal{G}(a\hookrightarrow(a\lor a^{\prime}))(b)\sqcup\mathcal{G}(a^{\prime}\hookrightarrow(a\triangledown a^{\prime}))(b^{\prime}))
.
First, we have
a\unlhd a\triangledown a^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{G}(a\hookrightarrow(a\triangledown a^{\prime}))(b)\sqsubseteq\mathcal{G}(a\hookrightarrow(a\lor a^{\prime}))(b)\sqcup\mathcal{G}(a^{\prime}\hookrightarrow(a\triangledown a^{\prime}))(b^{\prime})
, as required.

2.3.2

Least upper bound.

Let
(a,b)=({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})

and
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=({[\rho^{\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime}]})
.
Moreover, let
({[\rho^{\prime\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]})
be an upper bound,

i.e.
an element
such that
({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})\leq({[\rho^{\prime\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]})

and
({[\rho^{\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime}]})\leq({[\rho^{\prime\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]})
.
Let us show that

({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})\lor({[\rho^{\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime}]})\leq({[\rho^{\prime\prime}]},{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]})
.
First note that
{[\rho]}\unlhd{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]}
and
{[\rho^{\prime}]}\unlhd{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]}

so
{[\rho]}\triangledown{[\rho^{\prime}]}\unlhd{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]}
.

Moreover, we know that:

{[\rho\sigma/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}=\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]})({[\sigma]})\sqsubseteq{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}\ \text{ and }\ {[\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}=\mathcal{G}({[\rho^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]})({[\sigma^{\prime}]})\sqsubseteq{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}

Let
\alpha:=(\rho\sqcup\rho^{\prime})\Downarrow t^{\prime}
.
First we claim that
\alpha\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
.
Indeed,
\alpha=(\rho\sqcup\rho^{\prime})\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho\sqcup\rho^{\prime}

by Lem.
35
, and it is easy to check that

\rho\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}
.
What we have to check is the following inequality:

\mathcal{G}({[\alpha]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]})(\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma]})\sqcup\mathcal{G}({[\rho^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma^{\prime}]}))\sqsubseteq{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}

Indeed:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}&&\mathcal{G}({[\alpha]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho^{\prime\prime}]})(\mathcal{G}({[\rho]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma]})\sqcup\mathcal{G}({[\rho^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma^{\prime}]}))\\
&=&{[\alpha((\rho\sigma/\alpha)\sqcup(\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}/\alpha))/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}\\
&=&{[\alpha((\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})/\alpha)/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}&\hskip-85.35826pt\text{since $A/C\sqcup B/C\equiv(A\sqcup B)/C$}\\
&=&{[(\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})(\alpha/(\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}))/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}&\hskip-85.35826pt\text{since $A(B/A)\equiv B(A/B)$}\\
&=&{[(\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime})/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}&\hskip-85.35826pt\text{since $\alpha\sqsubseteq\rho\sigma\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}$}\\
&=&{[\rho\sigma/\rho^{\prime\prime}\sqcup\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}&\hskip-85.35826pt\text{since $A/C\sqcup B/C\equiv(A\sqcup B)/C$}\\
&\sqsubseteq&{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}&\hskip-85.35826pt\text{since ${[\rho\sigma/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}\sqsubseteq{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}$ and ${[\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}/\rho^{\prime\prime}]}\sqsubseteq{[\sigma^{\prime\prime}]}$}\end{array}

3.

There is an isomorphism
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)\simeq\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
of upper semilattices.

As stated, the isomorphism is given by:

\begin{array}[]{rclcl}\varphi&:&\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)&\to&\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}\\
&&{[\rho]}&\mapsto&({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\\
\\
\psi&:&\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}&\to&\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)\\
&&({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})&\mapsto&[\rho\sigma]\\
\end{array}

Note that
\varphi
and
\psi
are well-defined mappings, since their value does not
depend on the choice of representative, due, in particular, to the fact that sieving
is compatible with permutation equivalence (Lem.
41
).
Let us check that
\varphi
and
\psi
are morphisms of upper semilatices,
and that they are mutual inverses:

3.1

\varphi
is a morphism of upper semilattices.

Let us check the three conditions:

3.1.1

Monotonic.

Let
{[\rho]}\sqsubseteq{[\sigma]}
in
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
,
and let us show that the following inequality holds:

\varphi({[\rho]})=({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\leq({[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})=\varphi({[\sigma]})

We check the two conditions (by definition of
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
):

3.1.1.1

On the first hand,

{[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\unlhd{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}

since

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})&\sqsubseteq&\rho/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})&\text{since $\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho$ by Lem.~\ref{lemma:sieve_is_prefix}}\\
&\sqsubseteq&\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})&\text{since $\rho\sqsubseteq\sigma$ by hypothesis}\\
\end{array}

Note that this is garbage by Lem.
40
.
So by Prop.
9
,
(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})
is also garbage,
as required.

3.1.1.2

On the other hand, let us show that

\mathcal{G}({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]})({[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\sqsubseteq\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})
.
In fact:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathcal{G}({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]})({[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})&=&{[(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})(\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]}&\text{ by definition}\\
&=&{[\rho/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]}&\text{ by Lem.~\ref{lemma:garbage_free_garbage_decomposition}}\\
&\sqsubseteq&{[\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]}&\text{ since $\rho\sqsubseteq\sigma$}\\
\end{array}

3.1.2

Preserves bottom.

By definition:

\varphi(\bot_{\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)})=({[\epsilon\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\epsilon/(\epsilon\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})=({[\epsilon]},{[\epsilon]})=(\bot_{\mathcal{F}},\bot_{\mathcal{G}(\bot_{\mathcal{F}})})
.

3.1.3

Preserves joins.

Let
{[\rho]},{[\sigma]}\in\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
, and let us show that

\varphi({[\rho]}\sqcup{[\sigma]})=\varphi({[\sigma]})\lor\varphi({[\sigma]})
.
Indeed, note that:

\varphi({[\rho]}\sqcup{[\sigma]})=(\alpha,\beta)

where

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\alpha&=&{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\\
\beta&=&{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})]}\\
\end{array}

and

\varphi({[\rho]})\lor\varphi({[\sigma]})=({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\lor({[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\\
=(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\\

where

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\alpha^{\prime}&=&{[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\triangledown{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\\
\beta^{\prime}&=&\mathcal{G}({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow\alpha)({[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\sqcup\mathcal{G}({[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow\alpha)({[\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\end{array}

It suffices to show that
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta=\beta^{\prime}
.
Let us show each separately:

3.1.3.1

Proof of
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
.

The equality

\alpha={[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}={[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\triangledown{[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}=\alpha^{\prime}

is an immediate consequence of Lem.
44
.

3.1.3.2

Proof of
\beta=\beta^{\prime}
.

Note that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\beta^{\prime}&=&\mathcal{G}({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow\alpha^{\prime})({[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\sqcup\mathcal{G}({[\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow\alpha^{\prime})({[\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})\\
&=&{[(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})(\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))/\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})(\sigma/(\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}))/\alpha^{\prime}]}\\
&=&{[\rho/\alpha^{\prime}\sqcup\sigma/\alpha^{\prime}]}&\hskip-142.26378pt\text{by Lem.~\ref{lemma:garbage_free_garbage_decomposition}}\\
&=&{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/\alpha^{\prime}]}&\hskip-142.26378pt\text{since $A/C\sqcup B/C\equiv(A\sqcup B)/C$}\\
&=&{[(\rho\sqcup\sigma)/((\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime})]}&\hskip-142.26378pt\text{since $\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha=(\rho\sqcup\sigma)\Downarrow t^{\prime}$}\\
&=&\beta\\
\end{array}

as required.

3.2

\psi
is a morphism of upper semilattices.

Let us check the three conditions:

3.2.1

Monotonic.

Let
({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\leq({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]})
in
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}

and let us show that

\psi({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\sqsubseteq\psi({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]})

in
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
.
Indeed, we know that
\mathcal{G}({[\rho_{1}]}\hookrightarrow{[\rho_{2}]})({[\sigma_{1}]})\sqsubseteq{[\sigma_{2}]}
,
that is to say
\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}/\rho_{2}\sqsubseteq\sigma_{2}
.
Then:

\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}/\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}=(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}/\rho_{2})/\sigma_{2}=\epsilon

which means that
\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqsubseteq\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}
.
This immediately implies that

\psi({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\sqsubseteq\psi({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]})
.

3.2.2

Preserves bottom.

Recall that the bottom element
\bot_{(\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}})}

is defined as
(\bot_{\mathcal{F}},\bot_{\mathcal{G}(\bot_{\mathcal{F}})})
, that is

({[\epsilon]},{[\epsilon]})
.
Therefore
\psi(\bot_{(\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}})})={[\epsilon]}=\bot_{\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)}
.

3.2.3

Preserves joins.

Let
({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})
and
({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]})

be elements of
\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
, and let us show that

\psi(({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\lor({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]}))=\psi({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\sqcup\psi({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]}))
.

Let:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\alpha&:=&(\rho_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2})\Downarrow t^{\prime}\end{array}

First we claim that
\alpha\sqsubseteq\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}
.
This is because by Lem.
35

we know that
\alpha=(\rho_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2})\Downarrow t^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\rho_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}
.
Moreover, it is easy to check that

\rho_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sqsubseteq\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}
.
Using this fact we have:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\psi(({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\lor({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]}))&=&\psi({[\alpha]},\mathcal{G}({[\rho_{1}]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma_{1}]})\sqcup\mathcal{G}({[\rho_{2}]}\hookrightarrow{[\alpha]})({[\sigma_{2}]}))\\
&=&\psi({[\alpha]},{[(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}/\alpha)\sqcup(\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}/\alpha)]})\\
&=&\psi({[\alpha]},{[(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2})/\alpha]})\\
&&\text{ since $A/C\sqcup B/C\sqsubseteq(A\sqcup B)/C$}\\
&=&{[\alpha((\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2})/\alpha)]}\\
&=&{[(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2})(\alpha/(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}))]}\\
&=&{[\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}]}\\
&&\text{ since
$\alpha\sqsubseteq\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2}$,
so $\alpha/(\rho_{1}\sigma_{1}\sqcup\rho_{2}\sigma_{2})=\epsilon$}\\
&=&\psi({[\rho_{1}]},{[\sigma_{1}]})\sqcup\psi({[\rho_{2}]},{[\sigma_{2}]}))\end{array}

as required.

3.3

Left inverse:
\psi\circ\varphi=\mathsf{id}
.

Let
{[\rho]}\in\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(t)
.
Then by Lem.
43
:

\psi(\varphi({[\rho]}))=\psi({[\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})={[(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime})(\rho/(\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}))]}={[\rho]}

3.4

Right inverse:
\varphi\circ\psi=\mathsf{id}
.

Let
({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})\in\int_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{G}}
.
Note that

\rho
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage-free
and
\sigma
is
t^{\prime}
-garbage,
so by Lem.
42

and Prop.
14

we know that
\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}=\rho\Downarrow t^{\prime}\equiv\rho
.
Hence:

\varphi(\psi({[\rho]},{[\sigma]}))=\varphi({[\rho\sigma]})=({[\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime}]},{[\rho\sigma/(\rho\sigma\Downarrow t^{\prime})]})=({[\rho]},{[\rho\sigma/\rho]})=({[\rho]},{[\sigma]})
